The driver’s eye now responds gently to bumps, braking and cornering. Acceleration makes it lag back; braking brings it slightly forward with a small downward nod; cornering makes it lean outwards. The response comes from the physics body’s velocity at the seat, including angular movement, and has no invented idle or constant-speed bob. Settings on the title and pause menus offer Cockpit head motion On/Off, defaulting to On for older saves. Off restores the fixed eye and the existing vehicle tilt while retaining manual look. The preference is validated and saved.

**Timing and comfort.** Sample at the fixed physics rate, smooth acceleration in world space, transform it into the vehicle frame and use an exact critically damped spring. Render the eye offset with the same interpolation as the vehicle. Limits are 18 mm sideways, 12 mm vertically and 22 mm fore/aft, with at most 0.76° extra pitch and 0.57° roll. Lean stays in the vehicle frame while the driver looks around. There is no extra render pass: mirrors use the resulting camera as before. Physics sampling stops with pause; leaving the cockpit, disabling motion, recentering, teleports and vehicle swaps clear the state. A discontinuous position/orientation or long simulation step starts afresh. Vehicle geometry is unchanged.

**Validation.** The 33 targeted camera, motion and save tests pass, covering direction, bounds, rest/constant-speed stability, damping, interpolation, 30/60/120 Hz response, resets, invalid input, every cabin and manual look while Off. Browser checks cover an actual drive with nonzero bounded motion and live mirrors, the pause-menu Off control, reload persistence and the 390-pixel phone layout. The previous long-running unit suite overlapped these edits and used a cached pre-update camera tuning module with the new camera code; its two camera failures disappear in the fresh targeted run. The previous browser run also found that its Clear-weather selector matched the new Last pole clearing teleport; that selector now requires the exact button name. The new full suite runs after the finished changes. Publication follows passing lint, typecheck and build; wider results are recorded in the PR.

## v1.73: right-stick tools and the Expedition menu

The radial tool menu used the steering stick, separated the two diff locks and mixed recovery equipment with vehicle settings. It now uses the right stick, reserving the left for steering and plank placement. The menu samples the stick on the release frame: returning to centre and releasing L1 together cancels rather than executing a stale highlight. Back/Esc also cancels. Camera orbit stays gated while the menu is open.

Actions sort into contiguous families: front/rear/centre diff locks, transfer case/hubs, crawl and tyre pressure; winch, planks and recovery gear; repair, refuel and lights; then trailer hitch, straps, drums and trailer tyres when available. Disabled tools remain visible and show “Unavailable right now” when highlighted. Short labels sit under distinct icons, and the centre spells out the full action.

The owner selected Expedition from three working previews. The final menu uses dark annular sectors and amber highlights, independent of the driving HUD. The comparison styles and their settings/save preference are removed. Small touch screens use a grid; resize recalculates the layout, including the dense trailer tool set. The old oval layout and its CSS are removed.

`?tool-menu-preview` exercises the production component and input code against sample vehicle states, with optional trailer tools. It does not change the saved game. Browser checks cover twelve and sixteen tools, target overlap, disabled actions, touch selection, actual in-game right-stick selection, left-stick isolation, cancellation, release-to-use and camera isolation. Unit checks cover grouped ordering and right-stick deadzone/input separation.

The release is based on v1.72 and preserves its mirrors and cockpit motion, plus the transfer-case controls and nested tyre-pressure/recovery menus. Those submenus keep mouse/touch picks, right-stick selection and keyboard/controller confirmation.

## v1.74: road edges keep their shape at every distance

The owner, playing on a phone held upright on Low, saw jagged road edges and the road surface redrawing itself on approach. An evaluator's recording from the Paddock Road (near the "Paddock creeks" history card) showed a saw-tooth outline on the dark band beside the track from about 15 m ahead, smooth beside the vehicle.

**Reproduced first.** `scripts/playtest.ts` gained `view=portrait` (390×844, touch). On Low the Paddock Road drive showed the saw-tooth in every shot. Colouring the terrain chunks by detail level (a temporary debug build) showed that everything within about 60 m of the car, including the saw-tooth, was already drawn by the finest 1 m chunks, while driving and when still. The coarser levels were not the cause of what the owner saw near the car.

**Cause.** The dark band is a clay bank, the bare cutting beside a graded track. Its edge was drawn from a distance stored at each 1 m sample. That distance includes a term from the local slope, which jumps from one sample to the next, so the edge zigzagged by up to a metre or two at 1 m spacing. Seen low and 15 m or more ahead, foreshortening turns those wiggles into long teeth. Close to the car, seen more steeply, the same edge reads as a curve, which is why the edge seemed to redraw as the car approached. Further out there was a second fault: the 2, 4 and 8 m chunks kept only the edge distances measured at their own vertices, clipped 2 m out. Between vertices on either side of a 4 m road both distances were positive, so on the 8 m chunks the road thinned or disappeared, banks moved by metres, and the edge visibly changed shape at each detail swap (160 and 360 m on Low).

**Fix** (`src/render/terrainMesh.ts`, `src/render/groundMaterial.ts`, `src/sim/world/terrainGen.ts`):

- A bank is now drawn as how far it reaches beyond the track's edge, blurred over 9 samples each way (bank samples on the same side only), and the shader puts its edge that far out from the track's edge. The blurred reach changes slowly along the track, so the edge is smooth.
- Coarse vertices with no edge of their own get the distance they really are from the nearest track edge (the least of a nearby sample's distance plus the distance to it), with that track's paint colour and band, instead of a clipped value and the ground's colour.
- On the 4 and 8 m chunks each vertex holds two distances, from the track's left edge and from its right edge, which change evenly across the track, and the shader takes the greater. A whole road can then sit inside one triangle and still be drawn at its width. The side of the track is found from bounds on the centre-line offset, not from the single nearest sample, which wavered near the centre line. Where a second track is within reach, the vertex falls back to one distance, because interpolating two different tracks' edge distances would paint between them. The 1 and 2 m chunks keep one distance: their triangles are too small to hold a road.
- The generator now keeps each sample's offset from the track's centre line next to its edge distance (`TrackPaint.offset`), and keeps both at every clay-bank sample.

**Measured.** `tests/terrainEdgeLod.test.ts` samples the shader's paint and bank cover across every CREB track (53,796 points, every 4 m, out to 3 m past each edge) at each level and compares it with the 1 m mesh. Share of points that differ by more than half, before → after:

| Level | Paint         | Clay bank    |
| ----- | ------------- | ------------ |
| 2 m   | 0.05% → 0.05% | 1.4% → 0.35% |
| 4 m   | 3.4% → 0.8%   | 6.7% → 1.7%  |
| 8 m   | 31% → 3.2%    | 20% → 5.1%   |

What remains on the 8 m chunks is at track ends and junctions, where the fallback applies, and banks shorter than the chunk spacing. The same test checks that the 1 m bank edge moves on average less than 0.13 m from one metre to the next along the Paddock Road and the CREB Track (0.16 m before, 0.11 m now). Against the old code, the three level checks fail.

**Cost on Low.** The terrain has the same vertices and triangles; each vertex carries 27 shading values instead of 25 (about 8% more terrain vertex data). The same views before and after on Low, portrait: the chase view on the Paddock Road drew 160 calls and 361,765 triangles before and 162 calls and 363,893 after. A fixed view 700 m from the road drew 49 calls and 146,911 triangles before and 50 and 147,567 after. The small differences come from grass and vegetation timing, not the terrain. Building the terrain at load takes about 0.8 s longer in Node (1.8 → 2.6 s under the profiler), and the per-sample offset and bank cache add 2 bytes per map sample (about 16 MB on the CREB map).

**Not changed.** Potholes and other round patches still fade out on the 4 and 8 m chunks, which have no vertices inside them. That is a separate detail-level effect, and potholes there are a few pixels across. The track paint's deliberate ±0.15 m wobble (so the edge isn't ruled) is kept, and at a low angle it still shows as a slight ripple.

**The dark round patches** under and just behind the vehicle in the recording are tree shadows. With shadows turned off they disappear; the paint and banks stay. They are not mud holes or churn.

**Dev tooling.** `scripts/playtest.ts` (from draft PR #187) drives the real game along named tracks with the autopilot and saves screenshots and a log. This release takes it to `main` with the new portrait view.

## v1.75: the interface sized for the television and the phone

An evaluator playtested v1.73 on the owner's two screens: the television (the Electron app under `batocera/` full screen on a NUC, 1920×1080, read from the couch) and a phone (390×844 upright, 844×390 sideways, touch, Low). The owner's words: "the UI is too small or in the way or not user friendly." The television build expects a controller first (Batocera, `preload.js` watches the gamepad for Select + Start to quit) with the keyboard as a fallback; the prompts, history lessons, tool-menu hint, map hint and pause hints already follow the device last used (`InputManager.activeDevice`) and the chosen controller type, so they show the pad's buttons once the pad is touched and keys once a key is pressed. That was checked, not changed.

**One scale for the whole interface** (`src/ui/uiScale.ts`). Every length in the five stylesheets is now a multiple of `--u` (1 px × `--ui`): 1,186 lines were rewritten mechanically from `Npx` to `calc(N * var(--u))` (1 px hairlines and media queries left alone). `--ui` is 1 on a touch screen and on a window up to 1280×720, the size the layouts were drawn for, so phones and the browser tests' window look as before. Without touch it grows with the screen: 1.875 at 1920×1080 (13 px body text becomes 24 px, the usual ten-foot size at 1080p) and in proportion above that (3.75 at 4K, the same apparent size). The dash cluster has its own `--ui-dash`, 0.8 of that on a big screen (1.5 at 1080p), so the binnacle stays in its corner, clear of the car; its dial lettering's floor rose from 9.5 to 12 px, so it reaches 18 px there. JavaScript that placed things in pixels follows: the tool wheel fits itself using the scale, the crawl gauges' canvas is sized and drawn at it, and the map draws its names, icons, question marks, the car's arrow and the frame's lettering larger when the sheet is shown small. CSS `zoom` was tried first and rejected: it multiplies `vw`/`vh` too (79 uses), and `getComputedStyle` then reports unzoomed sizes.

**Settings → UI size** (title and pause): Small (0.8), Normal (the default, the scale above) or Large (1.2), saved as `settings.uiSize` (older saves: Normal).

**Television layout.** The action prompt moved from bottom centre (over the car's bumper) to bottom right, above the crawl gauges when they show. A few lines read from the couch are drawn a little larger without touch so they reach 24 px at 1080p: the road name, the clock line, a note's text and hint. The tool wheel shrinks to fit the screen's height (about 0.7 of its drawn size at 1080p), so its names and labels are drawn larger without touch (names 18.5, labels 14). A pause page scrolls to the focused item, for a controller on the larger lettering. The paper map's sheet is wider without touch (0.6 of its long side instead of 0.45) to give the larger names room; the scale bar moved outside the frame, where it can't sit under a place.

**The map's labels.** Names were kept clear of each other and of place icons, but not of the "?" marks or the car's arrow, and nearby "?" marks piled up. `placeMapLabels` now takes those as obstacles and scales its boxes with the mark size, and `mergeHints` draws one "?" for marks closer than 32 units.

**History cards.** On a screen without touch a card shrinks to its title once it has been up 8 s and the car is 40 m from where it opened (`drivenOff`), then closes 7 s later; before, the Daintree Village card stayed up for its whole 26 s reading time, at least 90 m of driving. Every note shown is kept in **Pause → Trip journal → Notes on the way** (the journal held only side trips before). The notices stack is hidden behind every pause page, not just the journal, so a card no longer shows through.

**Phone.** Upright, the prompt is one line in the band under the dials (it wrapped to two lines over the car, above the right-hand buttons), the panels under it move down a line while it shows, and a history note opens as its title only (it sat in the road ahead below the dials). Upright and sideways the dials are 78 and 50 px (68/46 sideways before) with a 12 px lettering floor, so the numbers read at 12 px and up. The pitch and roll panel is shown at 0.62 of its size (0.48 before) and draws its lettering at 19.5 units on a phone, 12 px on screen. The top row of buttons and the camera buttons are 44 px high (32 before; the dash panels under them start 12 px lower), pause buttons at least 44 px, and the remaining 9–11 px labels (GAS/BRAKE on the stick, the 4WD button's L C F R marks, the note's hint, the pause status labels, the tool grid's family labels) are 12 px. These phone sizes apply to the default Dash cluster; Edge lines and Field kit keep their phone layouts.

**The audit script** (`scripts/ui-audit.ts`, dev tooling from draft PR #188, taken to `main` here). Three changes. (1) Its two touch views stopped after the tool menu because the script pressed Escape to close the menu: a key press makes the keyboard the device in use, and the game then hides the touch buttons (by design, for a keyboard on a tablet), so `[data-tap="map"]` was never visible. A phone has no Escape key; this was a script fault, not a game bug. The script now closes the wheel and the map with taps and opens pause with the II button. (2) On the TV view it pressed and released Q, which opens the hold-to-use wheel and closes it again before the measurement, so the tools screen had measured the driving screen; it now holds Q through the capture. (3) It measured SVG lettering twice, once at its drawing-unit size (the "8.5 px gauge numerals" were 8.5 viewBox units, 11–13 px on screen) and once by its box height, and it ignored CSS transforms. It now measures text as drawn: font size times any transform, and SVG text's font size times the drawing's scale on screen. It also reports what share of each screen the interface covers, overall and in the middle third of the picture.

**Tests.** `tests/uiScale.test.ts` (the scale at each size, 4K, Small/Large, the dash scale, saving, the drive-off rule); `tests/mapLabels.test.ts` (names clear of "?" marks and the arrow at a larger mark size, merged marks); `e2e/uiSize.spec.ts`: at 1920×1080 without touch every piece of driving text is 18 px or more, prompts, note text and the road name 24 px or more, and no panel is in the middle third of the picture; at 390×844 and 844×390 touch, driving text is 12 px or more, every button 44 px, the upright prompt and note sit in the top 30% of the screen, and the pitch and roll panel's lettering is 12 px or more. A `setCrawlGauges` test hook forces the panel on. Audit numbers, before and after, are in the pull request.

## v1.76: the last small things on the phone

The v1.75 audit's phone views listed what was left: the map's Close button (58×32 upright, 61×36 sideways) and the title pages' ‹ Back (31×44) under 44 px on a side, and 10–11 px lines on the title, setup and briefing screens (the version, "Over the Cape" above a heading, the episode options' small lines, the build label). On a touch screen these are now 44 px and 12 px; the title screens come before the touch controls, so the rules follow `pointer: coarse`. The audit and the browser tests' text measure also stop counting a whole-pixel rounding of a box's height as a transform: a sideways phone's history note measured 11.9 px for its 12 px text.

## v1.77: the panels move down for every prompt on an upright phone

The touch browser test (`e2e/touch.spec.ts`, "the HUD's panels keep clear") caught a v1.75 fault: with two prompts up at once on a phone held upright (release the handbrake, attach the winch), the prompts filled two lines under the dials but the panels below moved down by one, so the warning lamps sat under the second prompt. The HUD now sets `--prompt-count` and the panels move down 33 px per prompt.

The same suite has three failures that also fail, identically, on the v1.74 build, so they predate v1.75: the landscape controls test waits for `.tool-ring`, which the v1.73 tool menu no longer has; the Joystick-only test reads full throttle after pulling the stick back; and the panels test expects one tap to close a history note, while a tap on a short note opens it to its full text. They are left for a separate fix.

## v1.78: the Hilux SR5 Xtra Cab

The owner asked for a new vehicle: a black 1985 Toyota Pickup (Hilux) SR5 Xtra Cab 4x4, built up like the truck in _Back to the Future_, with sourcing before any modelling. It is game id `xtracab`, "Hilux SR5 Xtra Cab" in the menu, with no film title, dealer sign, film plate or lamp maker's lettering anywhere in the game.

**Sources** (`reference/vehicles/hilux-sr5-xtracab-1985/SOURCES.md`).

- **Dimensions and running gear** come from Toyota's own 1985 Trucks brochure (a scan, linked, not committed), column "4x4 SR5 Xtracab Sport": 112.2 in wheelbase, 56.3/55.5 in tread, 184.1 in long, 66.5 in wide, 67.1–67.3 in high, a 75.0 in bed, 10.6 in running clearance, 4.100 axles, a 1.000/2.276 transfer case, 19.3 US gal, 116 hp at 4,800 rpm and 140 lb ft at 2,800. The W56's ratios are Wikipedia's. **The listing sites were wrong for this truck:** iSeeCars and CarGurus both give the 1985 4WD extended cab a 121.5 in wheelbase and 193 in length; the brochure has no such wheelbase in 1985. Their figures were not used.
- **Photographs.** Eleven Commons photographs of stock 1984–88 trucks are committed (CC BY 2.0 and CC BY-SA 4.0, credited in the table): a square-on side of a 4WD long bed, three Xtracab three-quarter views, the 1984–86 face, tailgates, and two interiors. **They are the 1,920 and 3,840 px copies Commons serves, not the originals**: Wikimedia refused every original from this container (HTTP 429, "use thumbnail images in sizes listed on…"), as it did for the MQ and the Range Rover. `manifest.json` has the originals' URLs. This goes against the folder README's rule (originals only); a scaled copy to compare against was judged better than none, and the files say what they are.
- **No square-on side view of an Xtracab exists under a free licence** that the search found. The side was laid out on two views of long beds, which share the Xtracab's 2,850 mm wheelbase: the Commons street photograph and the brochure's studio side view. The brochure's table then gives the differences (53 mm shorter overall, a bed 290 mm shorter, so a cab about 235 mm longer).
- **Film fit-out**: links and quotations only. The parts list is from Greg's Toyota Restorations, which restored the screen-used truck of the second and third films; shapes are from two replicas photographed on Commons (linked, not committed, as the owner asked for replicas). IMCDb's stills refused the container, so no film still was looked at. The table in `SOURCES.md` gives each detail and its source, and "Not confirmed" lists eleven points, chiefly: 31 inch tyres at stock height (the restorer) against 33 inch on a 2–3 inch lift (RealTruck's "best guess"); a single or a double roll-bar hoop; the rear bumper's shape, which no source that could be opened shows; manual or automatic front hubs.

**The body** (`src/render/xtracabBody.ts`, `xtracabGear.ts`, `xtracabInterior.ts`, `xtracabLayout.ts`, `xtracabPaint.ts`) is built with the extrusion helpers the LC79 uses. Stations from the front axle: nose skin −0.615 m, door 0.531 to 1.59, cab back 1.94, bed 1.96 to 3.915 (1.065 behind the rear axle). Heights above the hubs from the street photograph: sill 0.19, opening tops 0.537 and 0.528, bonnet edge 0.68, screen base 0.90, roof 1.345 (the published height less the stock tyre radius). The screen leans back 55°. Sheet metal is 1.62 m wide, the flares 1.69 m (published). It stands 1.74 m to the roof on the 31s. At the calibrated side camera (`studio.view(-40, 1.0, 0, 0, 0.9, 0, 8)`, wheels aligned, both 2.85 m apart) the outline lies on the photograph's nose, bonnet, screen, roof, door and bed rail; the cab back and tail differ by the Xtracab's 235 mm and 53 mm. 64.9k triangles with wheels and boards (44.8k body), under the 80k budget.

**Every part added, where it is attached and which way it faces.**

- **Spare:** flat under the back of the bed between the frame rails (which stand 0.89 m apart inside behind the axle, 51 mm clear of a 31 each side), outer face down, hub at z 2.02; hung on a rod from a crossmember above it, which stands on lugs on the rails, with a plate under its hub. Its tread is 0.40 m off the ground, the plate 0.35 m. Placed with `mountSpare()`; `tests/spares.test.ts` passes.
- **Front bar:** two tubes across the nose (0.275 and 0.105 m above the origin, 0.17 m ahead of the nose skin) that wrap back round the corners and stop 0.12 m ahead of the tyres, four plates welded between them, two bracket plates back to the frame horns. The hoop stands on the upper tube and leans back 30 mm at its top, level with the bonnet's edge.
- **Front lamps:** two rectangular lamps on stems from the upper tube inside the hoop, facing forward.
- **Winch (game addition):** on a plate between the two brackets, drum across the car, behind the tubes; its roller fairlead on a plate between the tubes, facing forward.
- **Rear bar:** the same two tubes, on brackets from the frame's rear crossmember; the rear plate on a backing plate between them, facing back. The front plate hangs from the front lower tube.
- **Tow tongue (game addition):** bolted under the rear crossmember, running back under the lower tube, the ball on top.
- **Roll bar:** an upright hoop behind the cab with its feet on plates on the bed floor; a second hoop from feet 0.2 m further back, leaning forward against the first at the top; a brace each side from the second hoop down onto a plate on the wheel housing.
- **Damper brackets:** the shared suspension code draws the dampers; their top eyes now meet a bracket under each front wheelhouse roof and a lug on each rear wheel housing.
- **Roll-bar lamps:** four round lamps on stems from the hoop's top, facing forward over the roof.
- **Mirrors:** a plate bolted to each door below the glass, a bent wire arm and a stay, the head facing back.
- **Aerial:** on the right front fender ahead of the screen pillar.
- **Recovery boards (game addition):** the boards are 2.4 m and the bed 1.9 m, so the two lie stacked on the left with their front ends on the floor at the front wall, resting on the tailgate's top, tails 0.45 m out over the back. Three straps hold them, one over the lower board and two over both, each ending at a tie-down ring on the floor. (Side by side they hid the roll bar from the chase camera.)

**Handling** (`src/data/xtracab.ts`). 1,850 kg as driven (the brochure's 3,400 lb empty plus two people, the bars, winch, boards and fuel), 55% on the front, centre of mass 0.72 m up; leaf springs with 0.12 m of droop and 0.09 m of bump (0.11 m on the earlier Hilux: the 31s reach the stock openings sooner); the 22R-E's curve through 190 Nm at 2,800 and 86.5 kW at 4,800; the W56, 4.10 and 2.276; 29° of lock for the 12.8 m turning circle; a front stabiliser bar; part-time with manual hubs; no snorkel (intake 0.98 m up). 60 km/h comes up in under 11 s, quicker than the earlier Hilux; it holds a full-lock turn at 50 km/h and climbs 30° in low.

**Sound, lamps, cabin.** Its own engine tuning, `efiFourSynthTuning`: the Hilux's four with a steadier idle, a quicker catch, a hushed intake, fewer pops on the overrun and a lower exhaust. The headlamps are the low and high beams; all six driving lamps come on with high beam. **Game liberty:** the yellow covers are drawn in the driving-lamp material so they glow; a real cover is opaque and comes off. The cabin is left-hand drive (as the Camel Trophy car is): grey cloth buckets, a console, the squared dash and two-spoke wheel of the interior photographs, speedometer, tachometer and a small gauge, three working mirrors.

**Unlock.** "Clear all eight gates on the scored rock climb", in any vehicle. The gate course (v1.68) fed no unlock until now, the starter 40 Series can do it, and it suits a truck built for showing off on rocks. The result card names the unlock. Test mode opens it as it does the others.

**A shared fault found on the way: every bent tube was drawn inside-out.** `sweptTube()` (behind `continuousTube()` and `VehicleBuilder.chain()`, so most bull bars, racks, hoops and tilt bows) wound its triangles facing into the tube. With one-sided materials the near half of each tube was culled and its far inner wall drawn instead: tubes looked pale and thin, and anything inside one showed through (on this truck, the plates between the bumper tubes stood out in front of them). The winding is corrected. **This changes how the other vehicles' bars look:** they now render as solid tube in their own dark colour. All thirteen were rendered front and rear after the change and looked right; no geometry moved.

**Checks.**

- `tests/xtracab.test.ts`: the brochure's figures; the unlock and the save; open wheel wells, leaf springs, the left-hand cab, dials in view, six driving lamps drawn where the glow is; the published width, height and nose-to-tail length; **every tyre clear of the body at full bump, alone and across an axle, at full lock both ways** (rays through each tyre from sidewall to sidewall); low and high beam; the sprint, the turn and the 30° climb; water over the headlamps stops it.
- The clearance test found two faults, both fixed in the body, not the test: the bed's inner wall hung 17 mm into the rear tyres at full bump (the wall now stands on the wheel housing over the wheel), and the inner corner of a front tyre met the wheelhouse wall and frame rail at full lock with the axle crossed (both moved 25–40 mm inboard).
- `tests/xtracabDrive.test.ts`, on the CREB map: it **climbs the knoll rock climb** in low range with both lockers and stops on it with open diffs; it **crosses the Daintree, Roaring Meg and Donovan's Creek** in the wet without drowning.
- `tests/solidAxle.test.ts` (its raised rear tyre clear of the bed), `mirrors`, `fuel` and `spares` take it in. `e2e/vehicles.spec.ts`: chosen on the welcome screen once unlocked, it drives off.

**The second look.** A reviewer subagent was given only the paths of 24 renders (the studio set and 16 close-ups) and the wording in `CLAUDE.md`. It listed nine things. What was done about each:

1. _A bar pokes out through each A-pillar._ Real: the vent-window divider stood 0.17 m higher than the glass at that point. It now stops at the glass's front edge.
2. _The rear window's glass does not fill its opening, seen from inside._ Real, and true of every window: the glass was inset from its opening and the rubber round it is one-sided, so untinted slivers showed from the cabin. All the glass (doors, quarter windows, screen, rear window) now fills its opening.
3. _The planks' front ends seem to hang above the bed floor, and the straps end on the floor with no anchor._ A new low render from inside the bed shows the lower board's front edge sitting on the floor's ribs, so nothing moved there. The straps now end at tie-down rings, and the boards were restacked as above.
4. _The spare touches or overlaps both chassis rails._ It did not, but the gap was 36 mm a side. The rails behind the axle moved 15 mm apart (51 mm a side), and the hanger crossmember got lugs down to the rails so what the spare hangs from can be seen.
5. _Thin pale rods at the axles with nothing at their ends._ The dampers and their mount struts, from the shared suspension code, whose top eyes hung in the open wheelhouses. The brackets above now meet them.
6. _The front wheel sits forward in its arch, and the bumper's end is close to the tyre._ The opening is the photograph's (its wheel sits about 20 mm ahead of the opening's centre) and a 31 nearly fills it, so the wheel stayed. The bumper's ends were shortened 40 mm.
7. _The stowed spare has wheel nuts and a hub cap._ It is a road wheel, as every vehicle's spare is. No change.
8. _A raised pale block in the bed's front-left corner._ The sunlit top of the left wheel housing. No change.
9. _The planks overhang the tailgate and are held by one strap each._ The overhang is the boards' length; they now have three straps.

**A second reviewer** was given the 26 renders made after those fixes. It found no part floating, edge-on or through a panel, and listed twelve smaller things. Four were acted on: the plate under the spare's hub hung 25 mm below the hub with air between (it now bears on the hub's face); the tail pipe had nothing holding it (a strap from the right-hand rail); the bumpers stood 63 mm outside the flares each side (now 53 mm, the least a tube that wraps round the corner allows); the tailgate's letters stood 17 mm proud (now 6 mm). The rest were checked and left: the spare's nuts and hub cap again; the lower board's own strap, whose band runs between the two boards so only its ends show; the bumper tubes' ends near the front tyres (0.10 m clear, and the clearance test covers the bumpers at full lock); the spare between the rails (51 mm a side); the boards beside the roll bar's braces (0.07 m from the nearest foot plate); a rear leaf spring's tail and the vent-window divider behind the mirror (both attached, dark on dark); and a "flat grey panel" on the cab ceiling, which is the passenger's sun visor.

**Left open, and not this truck's body.** With the same rays run against the suspension group, the front tyres at full lock meet the shared code's drawn front dampers and their mount struts, on this truck and equally on the red Hilux and the HJ60. The body test does not cover those parts. It is noted under the roadmap's "Body clearance through full suspension travel".

**Not done.** No SR5 badges or stripes; no bed mat (the restorer lists one); no mud flaps. The published track is kept although the film truck's wider wheels must have stood further out. The physics treats the hubs as manual. The scripted full-route drive (`scripts/creb-drive.ts xtracab`, the episode's storm) finished the CREB in 44.8 minutes on 23.5 L with seven winch pulls.

## v1.79: the SR5 Xtra Cab is open from the start

The owner asked for the Hilux SR5 Xtra Cab to be available from the start of the game. It joins the 40 Series and the Camel Trophy Land Rover in `starterVehicles` (`src/data/unlocks.ts`), and its challenge (clear all eight gates on the scored rock climb) is removed. The rock climb no longer opens anything: its result card shows the score, time and penalties only. A save that already lists `xtracab` as unlocked still loads; the entry is simply not needed. No vehicle geometry changed, so no renders or reviewer were needed.

Checks: `tests/xtracab.test.ts` now checks it is open with nothing unlocked; `e2e/scoredGates.spec.ts` no longer expects the unlock line; `e2e/vehicles.spec.ts` picks it on the welcome screen from a save with nothing unlocked.

## v1.80: menus that fit on the phone

The owner sent four phone screenshots from v1.79 and asked why the checks had not caught what they show. Three faults, each fixed in the stylesheet, and each now with a test that fails on v1.79:

1. **Pause → Vehicle: a description spilled onto the next button.** The pause card is a column that scrolls when its page is taller than the screen, and its rows were allowed to shrink to fit; a button with a two-line description shrank to 44 px and its second line ran over the button below. The card's rows now keep their own height (`.pause-card > * { flex-shrink: 0 }`, `src/ui/styles.css`), which fixes every pause page, not only Vehicle.
2. **A history note covered the end of the road's hint.** On a phone held upright the road name and hint (left, up to 64 % of the width) and the note (right, up to 52 %) start at the same height. While a note is open, it is now at most 200 px or 44 % wide and the road name and hint narrow to the space left beside it (`src/ui/hudStyles.css`, the end of the file), in all three HUD styles.
3. **The tool menu's bottom row sat under "Tap a tool to use".** At UI size Large on a phone about 384 px wide the grid fell to two columns, six rows deep, taller than the screen; the bottom row was hidden behind the hint bar until scrolled. The grid now always has at least three columns (`src/ui/toolMenu.css`), so the twelve tools fit in four rows above the bar.

**Why they were missed.** No test opened Pause → Vehicle; the vehicle tests only checked that the welcome screen's buttons fit across a phone. The phone overlap test did not include the history note, and checked the note only on its own. The tool menu test checked only that the grid appeared at 390 × 844 at UI size Normal, where it has three columns. And the screens were not looked at on a phone before shipping.

**New checks.**

- `e2e/toolMenu.spec.ts`: at 390 × 844, 384 × 785 and 360 × 740, at UI sizes Small, Normal and Large, every tool sits inside the screen and above the hint bar without scrolling, and no label is cut off. It fails on v1.79 at Large with Refuel and Lights hidden.
- `e2e/uiSize.spec.ts`, "every HUD style and UI size": with a note and the road's hint up, in each HUD style, at 390 × 844 and 360 × 740, Normal and Large, the note overlaps none of the dials, the road name, the hint or the mission; then in Pause → Vehicle no description overflows its button and no row of the card overlaps another.
- A test hook, `changeSettings`, changes settings as the Settings page does, so the test switches HUD style and UI size the real way (setting the page's attributes directly left the dials undrawn).
- The older tool menu test compared each wedge's rectangle; with sixteen tools neighbouring wedges' rectangles overlap by about 8 px though the wedges do not (it already failed on `main`). It now compares each tool's family, icon and name.

**Follow-up to the v1.80 tests (no game change, so no new version).** The note test first hung tapping a note that had already closed itself, and checked the hint only while its text happened to show. It now reopens the note before each check and also checks the widest the road name and hint may grow beside it. Run with v1.79's stylesheet it fails (Dash cluster, 390 × 844); with v1.80's it passes in all three styles at both sizes and both UI sizes. The unit tests all passed (971).

## v1.81: the Blender-built FJ40

The short-wheelbase 40 Series now draws its body and wheels from the Blender model `public/models/fj40.glb` (`src/render/fj40Glb.ts`), loaded once before the drive starts. The hand-coded cabin and dials, the roof rack the planks ride on, the roof spare, the aerial, and the moving axles and springs stay as they were. The model is in metres, nose to −Z, and is lifted 0.108 m so its hubs meet the game's. Headlamp beams come from the model's lamp centres when both are there.

It is the same starter vehicle as before (`shortie` in `starterVehicles`), so a new journey can drive it with nothing unlocked. The vehicle list calls it **FJ40**. `?fj40=code` keeps the old body. `?fj40paint=` followed by six hex digits recolours the paint (for example `?fj40paint=b22d1f`). If the file fails to load, the game uses the old body.

The blend file and build log are in `assets/vehicles/fj40/` and `tools/blender/fj40/`. `e2e/fj40Glb.spec.ts` checks that the shortie loads from the model with no console errors, and that another paint colour loads cleanly. `tests/unlocks.test.ts` still checks the FJ40 is open with an empty unlock list.

## v1.82: both 40 Series bodies, to compare

The owner wanted the new FJ and the earlier body in the game at once. They are two starters, the same truck:

- **FJ40** (`shortie`) draws the Blender model when `fj40.glb` loads, as in v1.81. `?fj40=code` still forces this one back to the old body, and a failed load does the same.
- **Short-wheelbase 4WD** (`forty`) always draws the earlier hand-coded body. Same mass, gearbox, springs, cabin and engine sound. It is open with nothing unlocked, listed straight after the FJ40. Finishing the CREB in either one still opens the 60 Series.

A new journey still starts in the FJ40. **Continue** still resumes whichever vehicle was saved. Pause → Vehicle switches to the other and restarts from the last camp.

## v1.83: the FJ40 touring kit, and the body sat on its axles

The Blender FJ40 (`shortie`) was looked at in the studio from the front, both sides, the rear, above, the driver's seat, a wheel, the bull bar, the snorkel, the rack and from under the tail. The earlier short-wheelbase 4WD is unchanged.

**What was wrong, and what was done.**

- The body had been lifted 108 mm so its roof matched the code car. The arches, which were drawn around the model's own hubs, then sat 120 mm above the game's wheels. The body now drops 12 mm, which puts those hubs on the parked axles (`LIFT` in `src/render/fj40Glb.ts`). The wheels sit in the arches and the tyres meet the ground.
- The rear spare, as modelled, covered the left tail lamp. It and its carrier move 220 mm inboard, so both lamps show.
- The stock bumpers and the little red reflectors that hung on them are not drawn. A bull bar and a rear bar take their place, and the two together flickered.
- The roof rack, the recovery boards and the roof spare were drawn for the code roof, 120 mm higher. They drop with this body. The Blender wheel's tread was still in the boards, so that spare sits another 45 mm up on its clamp.

**Touring kit, and what holds each part.** All of it is on the FJ40 only.

- Bull bar: the code car's bar, winch and shackles, ahead of the grille, with the front plate on the pan facing forward. TOYOTA is stroked across the grille opening, inside the hoop.
- Two driving lamps stand on short stalks on the outer tubes of the bar, lenses forward. They come on with high beam.
- Snorkel: the scoop is on the outer face of the left guard, behind the headlamp (the passenger side). The tube runs up the A-pillar, two clamps reach the pillar, and the ram head turns forward above the gutter.
- Mud flaps hang behind each tyre, the top tucked into the sill.
- Rear bar under the lamps, brackets back to the body, the plate on the right facing back, a tow ball in the middle.
- Jerry can in a cradle on the right barn door, the uprights set into the door skin, clear of the spare and the lamp. The can's long side faces out.
- Roof rack as before (feet on the hardtop), with the recovery boards, a strapped swag, a toolbox and the flat spare, white face up, held by the centre clamp. A whip aerial stands on the left guard.

The intake height is unchanged, so the snorkel does not let it ford any deeper than the other 40. The studio loads the Blender model before it draws the FJ40, so those photographs are of this body.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given the studio set and the close-ups and nothing else. It found the roof spare through the boards (the spare was raised, as above) and a red piece hanging under the rear corner (the old bumper's reflector, now left off). After that, the side view shows the spare on the rack and the low rear view no longer has the red piece.

## v1.84: the road no longer darkens ahead of the car, and menus a controller can use

The owner, after v1.83: the vehicles still have many visual faults, the track still redraws after the Daintree River, the menus are poor to look at and to use, most of all on the television with a PS4 controller, and all of it could have been found by looking more thoroughly. This version fixes the track and the menus' controller faults. The vehicles come next; `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md` holds what was found in the two reviewed so far.

**Why the earlier checks missed these.** `scripts/playtest.ts` stops at the Daintree crossing by default and saves still pictures, which cannot show ground changing ahead of the car. `scripts/ui-audit.ts` uses a keyboard, photographs eight screens and never opens a pause page or moves the highlight. Each check covered what had just been changed, on a 1280×720 window with a keyboard, not what the owner sees on his screens.

**The track: what redrew.** The v1.74 fix (road edges) was confirmed by a calculation and never by looking at the same view again. Two tests settled it this time. `scripts/approach-check.ts` parks the car and draws the same view at the quality setting's terrain detail and with every chunk at its finest: 0.0–0.4% of the picture differed at three points on the Paddock Road, so the terrain's detail levels are not it. `scripts/reach-check.ts` stands a free camera over a stretch of track and puts the car down nearer and nearer behind it: the camera does not move, so whatever changes is drawn according to how far the car is. On the Paddock Road nothing changed (0.1%). On the forested ridge track, 300 m along, 11.1% of the picture changed once the car was within 50 m: the road ahead lay in full sun and turned dark along a line.

**Cause.** The sun's shadows come from one map centred on the car, 40 m either way on Low and 55 m on Medium and High. Beyond it nothing is shaded. Grass under trees was already tinted dark where it is planted (`scatter.ts`), which is why the paddocks looked right; a road, rock or clay under trees was not. In forest, most of the road is in shadow, so the edge of the shadow map showed as a line 40 m ahead of the car with bright road beyond it, all the way up the range.

**Fix** (`src/render/shadowReach.ts`):

- Every drawn shadow fades out over the outer third of the map's half-width (`SHADOW_RIM`), in three's shared shader chunks, so the map's edge is never a line.
- The ground takes a second, soft shade from a picture of the map's tree cover seen from above: one byte for every 2 m square, full under a crown and falling away past its edge, built once at load from the tree list. Where a drawn shadow fades out the ground fades to that, read from where the shadow would come from (the crowns' typical height over the tangent of the sun's elevation, towards the sun, at most 45 m). A road under trees is now shaded at any distance, and the sharp shadows of trunks and crowns sharpen out of the soft shade near the car.
- It costs one texture read per ground pixel and no extra shadow pass. three r186 has a two-cascade sun light (`three/addons/lights/SunLight.js`) that would draw true shadows further out; it was not used, because its far cascade would put several times as many trees through the shadow pass on Low, where the forest's shadows were already a quarter of a phone's frame.

**Measured** (Low, 1280×720, share of the picture that differs from the car 120 m away by more than 22 of 255 on a colour channel):

| Ridge track, 300 m along | v1.83 | v1.84 |
| ------------------------ | ----- | ----- |
| car 70 m away            | 0.1%  | 0.1%  |
| car 50 m away            | 7.5%  | 0.1%  |
| car 40 m away            | 11.1% | 0.2%  |
| car 30 m away            | 11.1% | 0.3%  |
| car 15 m away            | 11.0% | 0.2%  |

What remains is grass moving in the wind. `tests/shadowReach.test.ts` checks the shader patch (and fails loudly if three changes the line it wraps), the cover under and clear of a crown, and the shift for the sun's position.

**Not changed.** Grass tufts, shrubs and rocks beyond the shadow map take no shade from the tree cover; only the ground does. The shadow map's size and reach are as before.

**The menus: what a walk with a controller found.** `scripts/menu-walk.ts` works every menu at 1920×1080 with a simulated PS4 controller, saves a picture after each press and records where the highlight went.

- Pause → Settings stepped through every option of every row: 33 presses of Down to cross 16 rows, and Left and Right did the same as Up and Down. Choosing any option redrew the page with the highlight back on the first item.
- Pause → Controls opened scrolled to its end with only Back to highlight, so the top of the table could not be read. The v1.80 rule that stopped rows shrinking had also stopped the table's own scrolling box shrinking, and the highlight on Back then scrolled the card.
- ○ on every pause page returned to Resume, and on the title's Settings to New Journey, not the item the page was opened from.
- At the television's scale the pages were taller than the screen: on the main pause page Settings was cut off and Controls and Main menu were out of sight; the title's Settings ran to three screens at 180 px a setting, with the "✕ Select ○ Back" hint drawn over its buttons. The compass sat on the pause card's corner and a prompt showed through its foot.
- Phone driving and Joystick side were offered on a screen with no touch.
- A setting's name was drawn at half the height of its options.

**Fix.**

- **Rows** (`src/ui/menus.ts`). Up and Down step through rows: a setting's options share a row, and so do buttons side by side in a grid. Left and Right change the setting (the highlight is always on the option in use) or move across a grid; ✕ presses a button and on a setting steps to its next option. A change redraws the page with the highlight and the scrolling where they were, which also stops a phone's Settings page jumping to its top after a tap.
- **Pages.** Each page is a heading that stays put, a body that scrolls and a foot with Back and the hint that stays in sight. The first row brings the top of the page back with it. With nothing to choose but Back (Controls), Up and Down turn the body's pages. Back returns the highlight to the row and column the page was opened from. The Vehicle page opens on the vehicle being driven.
- **The television.** A screen without touch that is 640 interface units tall or less once everything is drawn at its scale (576 at 1080p) is marked `data-short` (`src/ui/uiScale.ts`). There the main pause page's eight buttons stand two abreast under one row of readings and all of it fits; the title's Settings draws each setting on one line and fits the screen; the title pages' hint sits bottom left, clear of the panel. The compass and the prompts are hidden behind any pause page.
- **The highlight** is a ring as well as the bar, and the row of the highlighted setting has a band behind it, so it is not mistaken for an option that is merely switched on.
- **The title's Settings** opens on the first setting, not on Back; Left and Right change a setting there too; Back returns to Settings on the menu.
- The two phone settings are drawn only on a touch screen.

**Measured with the walk** (1920×1080, controller only): Pause → Settings 33 presses → 14 to go round; the main pause page 8 → 4 with every button on the screen; title Settings three screens → one; Back lands on the item each page was opened from on all six pages (v1.83: none).

`e2e/pausePad.spec.ts` drives the pause menu with a controller at 1920×1080 and checks the rows, the changed value staying highlighted, Back, the Controls page opening at its top and scrolling, and that the whole main page is on the screen. `e2e/uiSize.spec.ts` now compares the rows of the page's body.

**Still open** (in `docs/ROADMAP.md`): the journey page lists one episode to choose from and its vehicle chooser is a wrapping grid with locked vehicles mixed in; Pause → Vehicle is a long list of paragraphs; Pause → Map → ○ closes the map and the pause menu together.

**New tools** (dev only): `scripts/menu-walk.ts`, `scripts/studio-audit.ts` (24 large, tightly framed pictures of a vehicle, for a reviewer who also has its reference photographs), `scripts/approach-check.ts` with `approach-diff.py`, `scripts/reach-check.ts` with `reach-diff.py`, and `max=` on `scripts/playtest.ts`. Test hooks `terrainLod` and `trackPoints`, and `window.studio.bounds()` in the studio page.

**Follow-up to v1.84's tests (no game change, so no new version).** The unit tests all passed (985). `e2e/pausePad.spec.ts` first ran out of its 15 minutes on the Units row, with every check before it passing: at 1920×1080 the software renderer takes seconds a frame and the test waits four frames a press, and the unit tests were running beside it. It now draws the 3D scene at a third of its size (the page and its menus stay 1920×1080), as the menu walk does, and passes in a little over two minutes. `scripts/menu-walk.ts` looked only rightwards along a row of buttons and could not find Controls from Settings on the two-column pause page; it looks both ways now.

## v1.85: the FJ40, first fixes from the November audit

The owner asked for the FJ40 improvements to start. This version takes twelve of the fifteen faults `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md` lists for the Blender FJ40 (`shortie`), and part of a thirteenth. The earlier hand-coded 40 Series (`forty`), the ute and every other vehicle are unchanged.

**Before and after.** 24 audit pictures (`scripts/studio-audit.ts`) were taken before any change and again after, with the standard studio set and 23 close-ups, and eight more close-ups after the reviewer's findings. The photographs compared were `rear-34-left.jpg` (the nearest to a true side view), `side-1977.jpg`, `front-white.jpg`, `front-34-hardtop.jpg` and `rear-34-hardtop.jpg`.

**The driver's view and the glass (items 1, 2, 3 and part of 15).**

- The cause of "no horizon or sky": the cab was still at the code body's height. This body's roof and glass are 0.12 m lower, so the driver's eye was 13 mm under the top of the windscreen and the dash stood across the glass. The seat, dash, wheel, dials and eye now sit 0.12 m lower on this car (`FJ40_CAB_DROP`, and `vehicles.shortie.cabin` once the model has loaded). If the model fails to load, the code body is drawn and keeps the higher cab.
- The glass was also too shallow. On `rear-34-left.jpg` the door glass is 30% of the height from sill to roof, and on `side-1977.jpg` 34%: 0.42 to 0.46 m on this body. It was 0.32 m clear. The top of the tub drops 1.35 → 1.33 m above the ground and the white cap's lower edge rises 1.81 → 1.825 m; the window openings run 1.345 to 1.795 m (were 1.40 to 1.765) with a 15 mm seal (was 22 mm). Clear glass is now 0.42 m, and the windscreen's is 1.35 × 0.425 m (3.2 to 1; `front-white.jpg` shows about 2.9 to 1).
- From the driver's seat the horizon is 42% of the way down the picture, with sky above it; the roof lining fills the top 20% (it was 42%, and no sky).
- Each side has one window behind the door instead of two, with a bar for the fixed rear pane and a thin one where the sliding panes overlap, and the door glass has its quarter-light bar (`front-34-hardtop.jpg`, which is a rear view despite its name, and `rear-34-left.jpg`).
- The windscreen frame, wipers, drip rails and mirrors moved with the glass. The wipers pivot on the frame's lower rail under the glass. Each mirror arm starts on the door skin just under the glass and the head faces back.
- The dash now stops at the screen's inner face. It ran 0.11 m further forward, so once it was lowered it showed outside as a dark slab on the cowl at the foot of the windscreen. This was found in the close-ups, not in the audit.

**Item 8, tyres.** The wheel is built from the outer half of the model's wheel, mirrored. Triangles were kept or dropped by their centre, so half of each quad of the centre rib went missing: the see-through zig-zag. Every triangle that reaches the outer half is now kept and the rest of it is flattened onto the centre plane.

**Item 10, the black strip on each rear quarter.** It was the top of the cabin's box over the rear wheel, drawn for the wider code body and standing 15 mm outside this body's side. On this car the boxes stop at 0.735 m, inside the 0.775 m skin.

**The touring kit: where each part is attached and which way it faces.**

- **Snorkel (item 5).** A flat box on the left cowl side, over the vent louvres just ahead of the door (x −0.775 to −0.875, 0.91 to 1.18 m above the ground). The tube leaves the top of the box and runs up the windscreen pillar, leaning back with the screen and 55 mm ahead of its face, to a ram head that faces forward beside the roof cap. Two steel bands with a strap each hold it to the pillar's front corner. It was a box on the wing over the tyre and 1.2 m of tube through the air.
- **Roof rack (items 7 and 13).** This car has its own layout (`fj40Rack`). Six posts stand on the hardtop, reaching 30 mm further down than before. From each post an arm goes out past the roof's edge and straight down to a clamp on the gutter (x 0.762 to 0.794, the gutter's edge is at 0.763). A straight arm would pass through the roof cap, which the first close-up showed. The rack is 2.44 m long (was 2.02 m), from 0.2 m ahead of the roof's front edge to 0.05 m behind the tail, so the 2.4 m recovery boards lie inside it; they are 0.13 m further back. The boards are the length the game lays down on a crossing, so they were not shortened.
- **Roof spare (item 6).** Lying flat, face up, on its plate on the boards, 4 mm clear, held by the centre clamp. v1.83 had lifted it 45 mm off the plate, which left the air under it and the clamp's pin showing as a post. Placed with `mountSpare()`.
- **Winch (item 11).** The drum is sunk into the bull bar's pan, directly behind the fairlead in the bar's face, its top 35 mm above the pan between the centre hoop's legs. The motor is inside the bar. On top of the bar it stood in front of the left headlamp. Both headlamps are clear.
- **Driving lamps.** Each has a round lens facing forward, just proud of its housing. A square lens lay in the same plane as the housing's rim and flickered. Not in the audit.
- **Jerry can (item 12).** On the right barn door, its broad side flat against the door skin, standing in a tray that two flat bars behind the can bolt to the door, with a strap round it. Its back is 0.19 m off the door, level with the spare, instead of 0.36 m. It clears the spare, the tail lamp below and the door handle.
- **Exhaust (item 14).** In the model: a round 60 mm pipe, a muffler ahead of the rear axle, up to 0.62 m over the axle, down to 0.37 m under the rear crossmember, ending 5 cm behind the tail under the rear bar. It was a 48 mm straight pipe at 0.30 m with the muffler where the axle is.
- **TOYOTA.** The grille had two: the model's badge, and letters the code stroked over it in v1.83. The stroked ones are gone.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given the standard studio set and 22 close-ups (30 pictures) and nothing else. What it found, and what was done:

- The aerial's base hung in the air beside the bonnet, about 0.3 m above the wing (it had since v1.83). It now stands on the flat top of the left wing, facing up.
- The roof spare's clamp bar was shorter than the rim's opening and higher than the tyre, so it held nothing. The bar is now 0.44 m long, spans the rim and bears on the tyre's bead on both sides, under the T-handle.
- The jerry can's strap touched the door spare. The can, tray and strap moved 30 mm outboard and 20 mm up; the strap's near side is 23 mm from the tyre.
- The driving lamps' stalks passed through the bar's side rail and showed underneath. Each now starts at the centre of the rail.
- The door spare's tread stood 24 mm into the rear bar ("no daylight"). The rear bar, its brackets, plate and tow ball are 45 mm lower; the tread is 21 mm above the bar.
- The snorkel's head was a closed cylinder with its wide end to the rear. It is turned round, wide end forward, with a grey mesh disc in its mouth.
- Not changed: the winch's hook rests in the fairlead with no rope showing (the rope is drawn when the winch is in use). The door spare sits across the seam between the two barn doors, where v1.83 moved it to uncover the tail lamp, and its carrier is hidden behind the wheel. The left mirror is close behind the snorkel, not touching.

Each fix was photographed again close up and read.

**Still open on this car.** Item 4 (front wings too deep and round, tyre against the wing on full lock with the axle crossed), item 9 (rear arches: shape, lip, sky showing through), and the rest of 15 (black door hinges, no step, plain wheel discs). Found on the way and not fixed: the bonnet is about 7 cm high at the cowl against `side-1977.jpg` and `rear-34-left.jpg`. `reference/vehicles/landcruiser-fj40/SOURCES.md` now has this body's measures, and four captions there were corrected (`front.jpg` and `rear.jpg` are three-quarter views of a soft top; `front-34-hardtop.jpg` and `rear-34-hardtop.jpg` have each other's angle).

**Tools.** The model was rebuilt with Blender's Python module (`pip install bpy`, version 5.3.0) from `tools/blender/fj40/fj40_body.py`. Run unchanged, the script reproduced the committed model byte for byte, so the script is the source. `tests/fj40Cab.test.ts` checks the lowered cab. `tests/spares.test.ts` passes.

## v1.86: the FJ40's body shape, and its door mirrors

The owner, after v1.85: fix it until it looks like the real thing. This version takes the rest of the FJ40's audit list that is about shape, and one fault the browser tests found. Only the Blender FJ40 (`shortie`) changes.

**Compared with** `rear-34-left.jpg` (side), `side.jpg` and `side-1977.jpg` (front three-quarter), `front-34-hardtop.jpg` (rear three-quarter) and `front-white.jpg`, each put beside the matching studio picture before and after.

- **Ride height.** At the model's own height the roof was 1.95 m and the front tyres were 9 cm under the wings (item 4: "about 3 cm above the tyre" on full lock with the axle crossed). Toyota's figure for the 1970s hardtop is 2,000 mm, and `rear-34-left.jpg` shows about 13 cm over the front tyre. The body now stands 42 mm higher on its axles (`FJ40_BODY_LIFT`): roof 1.99 m, 13 cm over the front tyres. The cab, the glass, the snorkel, the rack and its load, the jerry can, the aerial and the mud flaps rise with the body. The bull bar and the rear bar stay where the winch and the tow point are. The physics is unchanged.
- **Bonnet.** 1.215 m at the cowl instead of 1.28 m, 1.175 m at the nose, so it is nearly level, as in the photographs, where its rear edge is level with the seam between the tub and the hardtop. A dark air slot sits in its front lip above the grille panel.
- **Front wings (item 4).** The top rolled into the flare on a 160 mm radius, which read as a round slab. It is 85 mm now: a flat top and an upright side.
- **Rear arches (item 9).** Flat-topped with radiused corners (a superellipse of exponent 4.6 instead of 2.7, 1.06 m wide and 0.87 m to its top) and no raised lip. A dark box behind each opening, closed on its inner side, stops the cabin and the far side showing through.
- **Hinges and step (item 15).** The door and barn-door hinges are body colour. The step under each door stands 85 mm out from the side (it was 45 mm and hard to see).
- **Door mirrors.** `e2e/mirrors.spec.ts` and `e2e/headMotion.spec.ts` failed after v1.85: no mirror was ever drawn in this car. The model's mirror glass is chrome, so it loaded as plain metal and the working mirrors found nothing to reflect in; this had been so since v1.81. The flat chrome face that looks straight back now loads as mirror glass (`markMirrorGlass` in `src/render/fj40Glb.ts`). Both tests pass. One line of `e2e/mirrors.spec.ts` expected a button's class to be exactly `on`; since v1.84 the highlighted button is `on focused`, so the check now looks for `on` among the classes.

**Where the moved parts are.**

- **Door spare.** On the left barn door alone, face to the rear, on the model's carrier plate and boss behind it: 0.19 m higher than modelled and 15 mm further off the door, so it stands above both tail lamps, across the foot of that door's window (as `rear-34-left.jpg` carries its spare) and clear of the door's upper hinge. v1.83 had moved it 0.22 m inboard to uncover the left lamp, which put it across the seam between the two doors where nothing could hold it.
- **Roof spare.** Unchanged in place, lying flat and face up; its plate is 0.62 m square (was 0.4 m), out to where the sidewall is widest, so the tyre is seen to rest on it.
- **Rear bar.** 20 mm higher than in v1.85, under the tail lamps, its brackets reaching the body's lower edge; the plate sits 12 mm higher on it so it no longer hangs below.
- Everything else that moved rose 42 mm with the body it is bolted to.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given the standard studio set and 16 close-ups and nothing else. What it found, and what was done:

- The door spare had no visible carrier and sat across the door seam: moved, as above.
- Thin pale slivers in the lower corners of the rear arches: the cut faces of the floor showed for 10 mm under the dark boxes. The boxes now reach 6 mm below the body's lower edge.
- A flat bar across each rear arch that stopped short: the code cabin's floor, 1.04 m wide, reaching into the wheel tubs. On this car it is 0.78 m wide between the rear wheels. The cabin's own boxes over the wheels are left out on this car too, since the model now has them.
- The roof spare looked held up by a block under its middle: the wider plate, as above.
- The rear plate hung below the bar: raised.
- Not changed: the pair of rods above each axle end is the shock's tower, two braces from the chassis rail to the shock's top eye, which the game draws on every leaf-sprung vehicle; the leaf springs' leaves are drawn apart for the same reason. The snorkel's box covers the left cowl louvres, where a real one sits. The front door's shut line is two fine grooves close together.

Each fix was photographed again and read.

**Still open on this car.** The wheels show a white disc on the inside as well as the outside, because all four share one mirrored shape that is not turned for the left side (item 15). The doors are shut lines on a flat side, with no rounded lower corners.

## v1.87: the FJ40's wheels and doors

The owner: finish the FJ. This closes the FJ40's list in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`.

- **Wheels (the last of item 15).** All four wheels share one shape, the outer half of the model's wheel mirrored, so the inside showed a second white disc with its own hub cap. The inner half is now dark, like the back of a wheel and its drum, and the tyre is unchanged. Because the wheel now has an outer and an inner face, the left wheels are turned half a turn about the vertical so their outer face points out (`handedWheels` on the vehicle's look, read in `src/render/vehicleModel.ts`). A turned wheel's own z axis points the other way, so the flat-tyre squash takes its axle angle with the other sign for those wheels. No other vehicle sets `handedWheels`, so their wheels are placed exactly as before. Checked in the studio with a new dev helper, `studio.flat(wheel, angle, drop)`: with the wheel turned one radian about its axle, the flat is at the bottom on the left front wheel and on the right. The roof spare lies white face up; the door spare is the model's own.
- **Doors.** Each front door was two straight grooves from the belt down to the sill. It is now one grooved outline with rounded lower corners (70 mm) whose foot is 45 mm above the sill, as in `rear-34-left.jpg`. Hinges, handles and the step are where they were.
- **Fuel filler flap.** An outline on the right rear quarter between the door and the wheel arch, as on the right-hand-drive car in `front-34-hardtop.jpg`.

Compared with `rear-34-left.jpg` and `front-34-hardtop.jpg` beside the studio pictures. No part of the kit was added or moved.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given the standard studio set and nine close-ups and nothing else. It found nothing floating, below the ground or facing the wrong way. What it did find, and what was done:

- The door's outline was bold at the back and foot but a hairline at the front. The front edge runs where the cowl covers the tub, and only the tub was cut; the outline is now cut into the cowl as well.
- The door spare stood a little outside the body's left side. It is centred on its door, 30 mm further in, inside the body's side.
- The line along the side at door-handle height was faint and broken. That groove, the seam between the tub and the hardtop, is 12 mm wide and 6 mm deep (was 7 by 4). It is still a fine line.
- Not changed, all drawn by the game's suspension on every leaf-sprung vehicle: the rear axle's centre is a ball with a steel ring on its rear face (the differential's cover), which reads as hollow from behind; the rods standing ahead of and behind each wheel are the spring hangers, which meet the chassis rail at their tops; the pair of rods over each axle end is the shock's tower.

**What still differs from the photographs,** none of it on the audit list: the grille is chrome bars with a badge, where cars from 1975 have a mesh panel; the barn doors have no pressed panels; the front bumper and rear bumperettes are replaced by the game's bars.

## v1.88: the FJ40's grille, back doors and seams

The owner asked for the three small differences left after v1.87. All three are in the model. The reviewer's findings then changed the back of the car, as below.

- **Grille.** A mesh panel with TOYOTA across it in chrome letters 58 mm tall, as the cars from 1975 have (`side.jpg`, `rear-34-hardtop.jpg`), in place of four chrome bars and a small badge plate. The mesh is grey wires 14 mm apart, 12 mm in front of the black recess.
- **Back doors.** Each barn door has the outline of its pressed panel in the lower half, inboard of the tail lamps and under the handle (`front-34-hardtop.jpg`). The right one shows either side of the jerry can; the left one below the spare.
- **Seams.** The line along each side at door-handle height (the seam between the tub and the hardtop) did not show from eye level: its walls face up and down and its floor was body colour. Every shut line (that seam, the door outlines, the filler flap, the barn-door seams and panels) now has black faces inside the groove, which is how a real shut line reads. The side seam is 4 mm wide and 6 mm deep, like the door lines; the 12 mm groove of v1.87 lost its walls to the body's bevel.

Compared with `side.jpg`, `rear-34-hardtop.jpg`, `front-34-hardtop.jpg` and `rear-34-left.jpg`.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given the standard studio set and nine close-ups and nothing else. What it found, and what was done:

- **Nothing visible held the door spare, and it crossed the seam between the doors.** It now hangs on a swing-away carrier, as the car in `rear-34-left.jpg` has: a bracket on the fixed left corner panel, a tube across behind the wheel between the door skin and the tyre, a link down to the hub boss the wheel bolts to, and a latch on the right door. The spare faces the rear as before. On a swing-away carrier it may stand across the seam, because it swings clear before the doors open.
- **The tail lamps were on the doors.** The doors' outer edges were drawn 0.73 m from the centre, outside the lamps. The real doors stop inboard of the lamps and the corner glass, which are on fixed corner panels (`front-34-hardtop.jpg`). The edges, their hinges and the seams above them are now 0.52 m from the centre.
- **Jerry can.** Its caps overlapped the foot of the window and its tray had nothing under it. With the door narrower it moved 90 mm inboard and 50 mm down, still broad side flat against the right door: clear of the window, the door's edge and the handle (which is 50 mm shorter). A stay runs from each side of the tray's outer edge back to the door.
- **Ragged corners at the front of the bonnet.** The bonnet's sides ended 9 cm behind the grille face and 15 mm outside the front panel, which showed as a flap at each corner. The front panel is now as wide as the bonnet.
- **Driving-lamp lenses read as empty rings:** they were cream, the body's colour. They are clear glass.
- **The rear plate hung below the bar:** raised 10 mm.
- Not changed: the left mirror is 47 mm behind the snorkel's tube, so the two overlap from the front without touching. A few faint marks on the side panels (a short line behind each door, a tick ahead of the right rear arch) are shading on the cut panels, not parts. The shock towers and the winch hook are as noted under v1.86 and v1.85.

Each fix was photographed again and read.

## v1.89: the FJ40's rear corners

The owner sent a picture from the game of the right rear corner, taken from behind and above, and asked how things were still being missed. Under the curved corner window the lower body stood out past the hardtop and its top showed as a black ledge.

**What was wrong.** The hardtop's rear corners have a 0.20 m radius; the tub under it had 0.06 m. At the corner the tub stood up to 58 mm outside the hardtop, and the top of the tub, which is dark because it is inside the cabin everywhere else, showed there. It had been so since the model was first used (v1.81).

**Why it was missed.** The standard studio set and the 24 audit pictures look at the car from the side, the ends, level three-quarter views and straight above. None looks down at an upper corner from close by. The close-ups in v1.85 to v1.88 were aimed at the parts being changed. The reviewer sees only the pictures it is given. So nothing had looked at this join.

**What was done.**

- The tub's rear corners have the hardtop's radius, so the two are flush. The cabin's hollow has round rear corners to match.
- The tail lamps were 130 mm wide and would have hung off the new round corner. They are 64 mm wide and as tall as before, on the flat of the rear face between the door's edge and the corner, which is nearer the real car's tall narrow lamps.
- The seam between the tub and the hardtop runs round each rear corner to the barn door's edge, instead of stopping where the side ended.
- The spare carrier's bracket reaches forward to the round corner's skin (at x −0.69 the skin is at z 1.85).
- `scripts/studio-audit.ts` takes four more pictures, one looking down at each upper corner from outside (`shoulder-*`), 28 in all. `reference/vehicles/README.md` says to open them after any change to a body's outline.
- The hardtop is body colour inside, as the real one is. With a dark inside each pillar was half dark and half body colour, with a curved edge between, which showed from the driver's seat as a dark wedge at the foot of the right pillar.
- After the fix the car was photographed from eight more places chosen for its joins (both front corners from above, the roof's front and rear edges, the left sill from below, the right rear from below, the front from below, the right side) and each was read at full size. Nothing else was found.

**The second look, and what it changed.** A reviewer was given 48 pictures: the standard set, twelve close-ups and the 28 audit pictures. It found the joins sound. It also listed, again, things earlier versions had recorded as "not changed". Those are now fixed:

- **Shocks.** On leaf-sprung vehicles the game hangs each shock from a tower of two braces off the chassis rail, whose top stood in the open arch with nothing above it. A look can now say where the body gives the shock a mount (`leafShockTop` in the suspension layout). The FJ40's front shocks hang from a bracket under the inner wing and its rear ones from a bracket in the roof of the wheel tub, with no towers. Other vehicles are unchanged.
- **Door spare.** v1.88's carrier latched to the right door, so it tied the body's corner to a door across the other door. The carrier is redrawn: its arm runs along the rear sill, under the doors and the tail lamps, from a hinge bracket on the fixed left corner to a catch on the rear bar; a post stands on the arm between the door skin and the tyre, up to a plate and boss the wheel bolts to. Nothing is fixed to a door. The spare faces the rear. The model's own door-mounted plate and boss are no longer drawn.
- **Recovery boards.** Their ends were 2 mm from the rack's end rails and looked run through them. The rack is 30 mm longer at each end (2.50 m), so each board end is 28 mm clear. The rack still stands on six posts with clamps on the gutters.
- **Gutter.** Each side's rail stopped square just before the corner window. It is one band along both sides and round the rear corners.
- **Barn-door hinges.** The lower hinge butted against the tail lamp's housing. The hinges are at 0.92 m and 1.24 m (were 0.80 m and 1.22 m).
- **Mud flaps.** Each hangs at the rear edge of its arch, 12 cm behind the tyre (was 6 cm).
- **A last review of the finished build** found nothing floating or through a panel, and three more points. The shocks' lower eyes were 0.63 m out on the axle, inside tyres whose inner faces are at 0.595 m (true of the towers before them as well); on this car they are 0.52 m out (`axleX`). The spare's post was a thin tube; the arm and post are 50 mm box section with a gusset each side. The snorkel's tube is 25 mm further in (0.825 m out), which leaves a gap to the left mirror's head that shows from the front.
- Still as it was: two faint vertical lines low on each side panel are shading where the model's panels were cut, not parts; they need the panel's mesh rebuilt and are left for now.

## v1.90: the Perentie, stage 1 of 3

The owner asked for the Perentie's audit list, in stages so that each is finished and shipped before the next. This stage is what could not exist: what holds the rack, the cans, the aerial and the spare, and the open rear corners. Stage 2 is the front; stage 3 is the body's shape. Only the Perentie (`perentie`) changes.

Before any change, the 28 audit pictures were taken, including the four that look down at each upper corner. Compared with `drawing-variants.png` (sheet A, the 110 GS soft top).

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.**

- **Roof rack (item 3).** Each of the two crossbars reaches out past the rolled side curtains (0.955 m out) to a leg that drops outside the canvas and cranks in to a foot bolted over the tub's capping rail. The legs used to stop on the canvas. The front crossbar is 90 mm behind the first bow so its feet land on the tub, behind the cab. The boards lie along the truck on the crossbars, as before.
- **Aerial (item 5).** On the top of the left front wing, ahead of the windscreen, standing nearly upright. On the tub's rear corner it passed up through the canvas roof.
- **Rear corners and tailgate (items 7 and 4).** The corner panels run from the tailgate's edge to the body side. They were 0.11 m wide at 0.79 m out, which left a 5 cm slit at each corner. The tailgate is 1.06 m wide (was 1.46 m), as the audit measured the real one. The shovel, pick and axe moved in to fit it, each still in two clamps; the plate is 80 mm further right.
- **Jerry cans (item 4).** One on each rear corner panel, above the lamps, its broad side flat against the panel, in a tray held by two flat bars bolted to the panel, with a stay under each side and a strap round it. They hung outside the body sides, which made the truck 2.2 m wide. The audit said the real truck carries them on the rear face. The photographs here do not show an Army GS from behind, so that is not confirmed; some Perenties carry them in lockers let into the body sides behind the rear wheels.
- **Reflectors.** Inboard of each lamp cluster, where the can does not cover them.
- **Spare under the rear floor (item 9).** It was there, tucked up between the chassis rails where it could not be seen from any side, with its front edge inside the differential. It hangs 0.2 m lower and 80 mm further back, flat, outer face down, in a cradle of two rails and two crossbars hung from the chassis rails by four hangers. Its tread passes under the rear crossmember and shows below it from behind. Placed with `mountSpare()`; `tests/spares.test.ts` passes (the first cradle stood 2 cm into the tyre and the test caught it).

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 46 pictures (the standard set, ten close-ups and the 28 audit pictures) and nothing else. It found nothing floating, upside down or below the ground. What it did find, and what was done:

- The rear crossbar's feet shared a spot on the tub's rail with a canvas bow's foot. The crossbar is 0.1 m behind that bow now, as the front one already was.
- A 4 cm slot was open between the tailgate's foot and the rear crossmember. A sill closes it.
- The bonnet spare stood on a 0.36 m plate under its middle. The plate is 0.66 m square, out to where the sidewall is widest; the spare still lies flat, face up, under its centre clamp.
- The shovel, the pick's head and the reflectors were hard against the cans' bars and stays. Each moved 20 to 30 mm clear.
- The exhaust's tip ended beside the cradle's left rear hanger. It runs on 0.15 m to end under the rear crossmember.
- Left for the later stages: a pale wedge above each front tyre inside the arch (stage 3, with the arches); two grey blocks at the foot of the windscreen, which are its hinges. The spare's cradle is the lowest thing behind the rear axle, 0.2 m off the ground, as the real one is.

Each fix was photographed again and read.

## v1.91: the Perentie, stage 2 of 3 (the front)

Items 6 and 8 of the Perentie's list. Only the Perentie changes.

**The photographs were mislabelled.** Seven files named `*county*` were captioned as ex-Army County wagons, to be used for the front clip only. Opened one by one, they are two Army GS soft tops in camouflage (a 1988 truck, 63A, and a 1990 one): the truck the game copies, from the front quarters, the rear quarters and directly behind. `SOURCES.md` now says what each shows. They settle the open question from v1.90: `front-34-left-county.jpg` and `front-34-county.jpg` show a jerry can on each rear corner panel above the lamps, where v1.90 put them.

**Compared with** `side-right-county-1990.jpg`, `rear-34-left-county.jpg`, `front.jpg` and sheet A of `drawing-variants.png`.

- **Nose (item 8).** On the drawing the grille's face is 35 px ahead of the front axle at 14.55 mm a pixel: 0.51 m. The model's was 0.62 m. `FRONT_FACE` moves from −2.02 to −1.90; the bonnet, wings, lamps, chassis horns, front crossmember, steering box and bonnet spare move with it. The winch's rope leaves 0.12 m further back (`winchMount` z −2.13) and the headlamp beams start at the new lamps.
- **Grille (item 6).** The body's colour, seven chunky louvres over a shaded recess. It was a black panel of fourteen fine slats with two black uprights.
