The Troop Carrier now uses that code's bracket mounts, as the Hilux and the FJ40 do: each shock hangs from a bracket under its wheelhouse roof (0.385 m), its lower eye 0.52 m out on the axle, 7.5 cm inboard of the tyre.
- **Grey rods under the rear with an end in nothing** (there before). It was the tailpipe: its last length ran out through the right chassis rail to the corner, and the length before it through the rear axle tube. It now climbs over the axle inside the rail, drops under the two rear crossmembers and ends under the rear bar.
- While checking those, the top of each rear opening also showed the light grey of the body's inside behind the tyre (there before): the rear wheelhouses had no ends. Each now has a dark wall at its front and back.
- Not changed: **two short grey dashes on the left side ahead of the rear opening**, in one picture only (a side view from 2.5 m). They are in the same place, unchanged, in a picture taken from `main` before this version, and do not show from nearer; they look like the hairline v2.09 found on the Hilux's doors and could not trace. **The boards overhang the rack** 0.19 m at each end, as since v2.08; they are strapped on the rack. The muffler under the floor still passes through the crossmember at its front end, seen only from directly below.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the full standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` passes.

78.9k triangles with wheels and boards (body 64.6k; it was 76.2k and 61.9k).

## v2.12: the 40 Series ute

The owner asked for the 40 Series ute next, then the 80 Series. Only the ute (`ute40`) changes. Its folder has no photographs, and its cab is the game's 40 Series cab, so its 28 audit pictures and the standard set were compared with the FJ40 photographs (`side.jpg`, `front-34-hardtop.jpg`, `rear-34-left.jpg`, `side-1977.jpg` in `reference/vehicles/landcruiser-fj40/`) and with the FJ40's own fixes since v1.85. Commons has six photographs of stock Australian HJ47 and FJ45 utes, which would check the cab back and the tray, but it refused every request from the container for a file, a scaled copy or the file details (HTTP 429), so none could be added; their names are in the ute's `SOURCES.md`. The list is in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`.

- **Snorkel (item 1).** Its box now sits on the left cowl side over the louvres, just ahead of the door, where the air goes in to the engine bay. The tube leaves its top and climbs beside the windscreen frame, leaning back with it, into a ram head facing forward at the roof's front corner, held by two clamps strapped to the frame. Until now it left the guard over the front tyre and crossed 0.5 m of air to the pillar.
- **Winch (item 2).** Inside the bar's pan, as on the other winch bars since v1.94: what shows is its control box on the pan's top and the roller fairlead in the face. Its drum stood on the pan in front of the grille.
- **Door glass (item 3).** The foot of the door glass is 6 cm lower (`DOOR_WIN_LOW`, 0.19 m above the belt crease): on the FJ40 photographs the glass is about a third of the door's height. The quarter-vent bar is as long as the glass. The rear window keeps its height.
- **Underneath (item 4).** The exhaust runs back inside the right chassis rail, climbs over the rear axle and ends under the rear bar; it ran inside the rail, through the axle tube and out through the rail behind the wheel. The fuel tank and the muffler sit between the crossmembers at 0.3 and 1.0 m; the tank ran into the second.

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.** The snorkel's box lies against the left cowl side, facing out; its tube is clamped to the windscreen frame by two bands with straps; the head faces forward at the roof's front corner. The winch's control box sits on the pan's top between the hoop's legs. The front plate lies on the pan's face, facing forward, between the fairlead and the left shackle. Each rope over the load is hooked to the deck on each side. The high-lift jack is strapped to the headboard's right upright, its foot on the deck. Each outrigger is bolted to a front chassis rail and carries a shock tower and a spring hanger. The exhaust hangs inside the right rail. Neither spare moved: the tray spare lies flat on the deck under its clamp bar, face up; the other lies flat under the tray between the runners, face down.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 57 pictures and nothing else: the standard set, the 28 audit pictures and 21 close-ups (the snorkel from the side, ahead, behind, above, low, at its head and at its box; the bar from ahead, a corner, above and low; each door; the door from inside; the exhaust from low, the side, ahead, behind and at the axle; the tank from low and the side). It found nothing below the ground, and the tray spare, beacon, aerial, mirrors, lamps and rack attached. What it found, and what was done:

- **The ropes over the load were loose pieces** (there before): one end stopped part-way down the load's side, the other came out through the drop side, and from above nothing crossed the load. Each rope now runs from a hook on the deck up the load's side, over its top and down to a hook inside the drop side. Its top had run just inside the tarp.
- **The exhaust ran into the top of the rear differential.** This version's first route climbed only to the axle tube's height. It now climbs to 0.12 m over the axle, clear of it at full bump, and drops back under the slung spare.
- **The rear tyres touched the tray's underside, from the side** (there before). In plan they never met: the tray's frame rail under each drop side is outboard of the tyre, but as deep as the crossmembers it hid the tyre's top. It is 6 cm shallower; from the side the tyres now show 2 cm below it.
- **A grey A-shaped strut in each front wheel arch, standing on nothing** (there before). It was the shared suspension's shock tower, whose two braces start 0.47 m out; the ute's front rails are 0.3 m out. An outrigger from each front rail now carries each tower, and each front spring hanger, which stood in the air too.
- **The high-lift jack's foot and climbing block touched the cab's back** (there before). They stop 2.5 cm short of it.
- **From inside, the sky showed at both edges of the left door.** At the front, a slot between the windscreen frame and the panel round the door glass: the panel's front edge now meets the frame's back face. At the back it was the cab's rear corner, whose curved panel shows only from outside (there before): it has an inside face now, front and back corners on both sides.
- **The front plate hung below the bar's pan, against the left shackle** (there before). It is on the pan's face, 3 cm clear of the shackle.
- Not changed: **a tan rim under the tray, seen from below**, is the first spare, slung under the tray face down, not the tray spare showing through the deck. **The left mirror's arm and stay against the snorkel** are a picture overlap: from above, the mirror head is 3 cm outboard of the snorkel's box and 2.5 cm behind it, and the arms 4 to 8 cm behind the tube. One close-up meant to show the exhaust over the axle showed only the wheel; it was taken again from low and from behind.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` passes.

53.8k triangles with wheels and boards (body 38.9k; it was 53.4k and 38.6k).

## v2.13: the 80 Series

The owner asked for the 80 Series after the ute. Only the 80 Series (`sooty80`) changes. Its 28 audit pictures and the standard set were compared with `front.jpg`, `rear.jpg`, `side-left.jpg`, `front-34-left.jpg`, `grille-early.jpg`, `gxl-australian-front.jpg` and the two touring cars in `reference/vehicles/landcruiser-80-sooty/`. The factory body, drawn without the touring gear, was photographed again at the calibrated camera in that folder's `SOURCES.md` and laid over `side-left.jpg`: it still matches to a few centimetres along the roof, the screen, the tail, the flares and the bumpers, so nothing in the body's shape moved. No photograph of the real Sooty MkII may be committed, so the touring gear is still checked against its written descriptions only. The list is in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`.

- **Winch (item 1).** Inside the bar's pan, as on the other winch bars: what shows is its control box on the pan's top between the driving lamps, and the pierced hawse fairlead in the face. Its drum, drawn as a stack of rings, and its red motor stood on the pan in front of the grille.
- **Grille (item 2).** The 1990–94 pattern, because MY4X4 gives Sooty MkII as a 1993 car: a chrome surround, three slats above and three below a grey bar across its middle, TOYOTA in chrome letters on the bar, a small turbo badge at the bar's left end, and five uprights (`front.jpg`, `grille-early.jpg`). It was the 1995-on grille with the oval badge, as on the Australian GXL. The driving lamps still stand in front of it.
- **Exhaust (item 3).** On the right: under the floor, over the rear axle between the differential and the coil spring, and out behind the right rear wheel under the bar, as on `rear.jpg`. It ran on the left.

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.** The winch's control box sits on the bar pan's top, between the two driving lamps' stalks. The grille is on the front panel between the headlamp bezels, facing forward; its letters read left to right from the front. The exhaust hangs from three rubber hangers: one to the cab floor, one to the load floor over the rear axle, and one from the tail to the right chassis rail's corner. The snorkel rises out of its base on the right wing, bends out to the windscreen pillar and climbs it to the head, held by two clamps strapped to the pillar. Each door trim lies against the inside of a front door below the glass, with an armrest; each kick panel against the body side under the dash's end; each sill capping along the inside of a door under the glass. The swing-away spare is unchanged and still faces back, its arm's lower run now in the middle of its hinge post.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 50 pictures and nothing else: the standard set, the 28 audit pictures and 14 close-ups (the grille from ahead, each side and low; the bar from above, the side and low; the winch box; the exhaust from low, the side, behind, ahead, at the axle and at its tail). It found the wheels, the spare, the rack and its load, the light bar, the aerial, the driving lamps and the snorkel's brackets all in place, and nothing on or below the ground. What it found, and what was done (all there before this version):

- **The scrub rails stopped just short of the bull bar's side uprights**, a sliver of background between them. Each rail now starts inside its upright.
- **The snorkel's lower end did not meet its base**, and a sharp sliver stuck out of its first bend. The pipe ran into the top of the base nearly level, its open end showing, and its tight bends pinched. It now rises straight out of the base and turns out to the pillar in round bends; the clamps moved with it.
- **The spare carrier's arm ended in a needle point on its post.** Its lower run lay at the post's back edge and showed through the post's rounded corner as a sliver. It is in the middle of the post now.
- **The ground showed under the dash from the driver's seat.** The footwells had no front wall; they have one now, behind the dash.
- **A thin brown line ran along the inside of the right door.** It was the seam between the lower body and the glasshouse, at the waist, with the ground showing through. A capping strip runs along the inside of each door's sill, and each front door has a dark trim panel below it and a kick panel ahead of it (the doors were bare body colour inside).
- **A grey tube ended in mid-air under the floor.** The rear prop shaft started 2.5 cm behind the transfer case; it starts inside it.
- **The exhaust crossed the right rear shock and had nothing holding it up.** At x 0.27 it ran 1.5 cm into the shock; at x 0.23 it clears it by 2.5 cm and the differential too, and the three hangers above hold it.
- Not changed: **a small tan patch at the foot of the windscreen beside the snorkel**, seen from the right side. It was there before, does not show from the front or from the seat, and could not be traced from the close-ups. **A black rod from behind the right rear tyre to above the exhaust** is the shared suspension's rear radius arm.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` and `tests/sooty80.test.ts` pass.

71.7k triangles with wheels and boards (body 50.9k; it was 71.2k and 50.5k).

## v2.14: the 60 Series

The owner asked for the 60 Series, the MQ Patrol and the Range Rover next, each to be shown before and after. Only the 60 Series wagon (`hj60`) changes. Its 28 audit pictures and the standard set were compared with `front-34-left-n-hj60v-std-1987-standard-roof.jpg`, `rear-34-left-n-hj60v-std-1987-barn-doors.jpg`, `front-34-left-customwagon-1985.jpg` and `side-left-j60-mod-roof-tent.jpg` in `reference/vehicles/landcruiser-60/`. The side was laid over the last of these in v1.10 and its outline, windows, pillars, creases and arches still agree, so the body's shape did not move. The list is in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`.

- **Winch (item 1).** Inside the bar's pan, as on the other winch bars: what shows is its control box on the pan's top between the hoop's legs, and the roller fairlead in the face. Its drum, motor and gearbox stood on the pan in front of the grille.
- **Headlamp surrounds (item 2).** Each round lamp sits in a black square surround with a thin chrome strip round its edge, as on the cream 1985 Customwagon; the Japanese Standard's are plain black. They were solid chrome and read from any distance as pale grey squares.
- **Grille (item 3).** Its six horizontal bars are a dark grey a shade lighter than the recess behind them, so they show behind TOYOTA as they do on the photographs. They were the recess's own black.
- **Exhaust (item 4).** Down the right side inside the frame rail, under the front crossmembers, the muffler under the cab floor, up over the rear axle and over the two rear crossmembers under the load floor (clear above the slung spare), down behind the last crossmember, and out under the right end of the rear bar. It ran straight back at the axle's height, through the top of the axle tube and through three crossmembers.

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.** The winch's control box sits on the bar pan's top. Each lamp surround lies on the grille band, facing forward, its chrome edge on its face. The exhaust hangs inside the right rail: the muffler from a rubber hanger to the cab floor, the run over the axle from one to the load floor; its tail points down and out under the rear bar. Neither spare moved: one lies flat under the back on its cradle, face down; the other lies flat on the rack, face up.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 49 pictures and nothing else: the standard set, the 28 audit pictures and 13 close-ups (the front from ahead and each corner, a headlamp from ahead and the side, the grille from low, the bar from above and low, the winch box, the exhaust at the axle, from low, behind and the side). It found the bar, lamps, mirrors, aerial, rack, both spares and all four mud flaps attached, and nothing below the ground. What it found, and what was done:

- **The tailpipe looked like a loose piece, with nothing joining it forward** (there before). The pipe ran into the rear crossmember and out of the other side, so from below its tail stopped at the crossmember. Rerouted as above, over the rear crossmembers.
- **The rack's boards stood 5 cm past its front rail** (there before). They are 7 cm further back, inside it.
- Not changed: **the rear mud flap resting on the test block** in the picture with the axles crossed: it is a rubber flap brushing the rock the wheel is climbing. **The front tyre filling its opening** in the same picture: a closer look shows a dark gap between the tread and the opening's top. **The roof spare over the rack's rear rails from above** is perspective; from the side there is a gap of about 9 cm.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` and `tests/hj60.test.ts` pass.

78.5k triangles with wheels and boards (body 59.5k; it was 77.8k and 58.8k).

## v2.15: the MQ Patrol

The second of the three the owner asked for. Only the MQ Patrol hardtop (`mq`) changes. Its folder still has no photographs: Commons answered every request from the container with HTTP 429 again, for several hours, as it did when the body was built in v1.55. So its 28 audit pictures and the standard set were compared with what its `SOURCES.md` records of the photographs and checked for faults a render shows on its own. The list is in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`.

- **Winch (item 1).** Inside the bar's pan, as on the other winch bars: what shows is its control box on the pan's top and the roller fairlead in the face. Its drum, motor and gearbox stood in a cradle on the pan in front of the grille.
- **Exhaust (item 2).** Down the right side inside the frame rail, under the crossmembers, the muffler under the cab floor between two crossmembers, up over the rear axle under the load floor, and out under the rear bar pointing back. It ran at the axle's height, through the top of the axle tube and through three crossmembers, and the muffler ran into a crossmember.
- **Battery box (item 3).** The 24 V system's second battery box is against the outside of the left chassis rail, its top under the cab floor. It stood 1 cm off the rail with nothing holding it, and its top rose through the floor.

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.** The winch's control box sits on the bar pan's top. The exhaust hangs inside the right rail: the muffler from a rubber hanger to the cab floor, the run over the axle from one to the load floor; its tail points back under the bar, 0.2 m inboard of the right recovery point. The battery box is bolted to the left rail's outer face. The dash and its pad stand between the A-pillars, behind the glass. Neither spare moved: one hangs on the swing-away carrier on the rear bar, facing back; the other lies flat on the rack, face up.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 49 pictures and nothing else: the standard set, the 28 audit pictures and 13 close-ups (the front from ahead and a corner, the bar from above and low, the winch box, the exhaust from low, ahead, behind, the side and at the axle, the battery box from low and the side, the cab floor). It found the wheels, both spares, the bar, the mud flaps, the recovery points, the rack and its load, the mirrors and the aerial attached, and nothing below the ground. What it found, and what was done (all there before this version):

- **The dashboard showed outside, past the windscreen and both A-pillars.** Its padded top ran 8 cm forward of the leaning glass and both it and the dash panel stood out past the pillars, so their ends showed above the wings, and the dial hood ran into the right pillar from the seat. The dash and its pad are 1.44 m wide, behind the glass; the hood stops 4 cm short of the pillar.
- **A thin sliver hung under the lower front corner of each wing.** The front clip's outline ran 2 cm below its bottom edge and back up the same line, and the panel's bevel drew that as a sliver. The outline starts at the bottom edge now.
- **The tailpipe's end sat close under the right rear recovery point.** It ended pointing out at x 0.56; it now ends pointing back at x 0.44.
- **The exhaust seemed to meet the rear axle's differential.** A picture from directly under the axle shows the pipe 0.23 m from it; the first route had run through the axle tube, which this version had already changed.
- Not changed: **the differential looks like an open drum from behind**; from below it is closed. It is the shared suspension's part, drawn the same on every vehicle. **The roof spare reaching the rack's right rail from above** is perspective: from the side and behind the rails pass outside it. **The door spare covering a corner of the rear window** and **the carrier's post outboard of the tail lamp** are the swing-away carrier as built in v1.60. **The NISSAN letters half hidden by the bull bar's crossbar from dead ahead** read fully from a little above or below.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` and the MQ's tests pass.

## v2.16: the Range Rover

The last of the three the owner asked for. Only the Range Rover two-door (`rangie`) changes. Its folder has no photographs either: Commons refused its fourteen files again (HTTP 429), as when it was built in v1.58. So its 28 audit pictures and the standard set were compared with what its `SOURCES.md` records of the photographs and checked for faults a render shows on its own. The list is in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`.

- **Winch (item 1).** Inside the roo bar's pan, as on the other winch bars: what shows is its control box on the pan's top and the roller fairlead in the face. Its drum, motor and gearbox stood on the pan in front of the grille.
- **Exhaust (item 2).** Down the left side inside the frame rail, under the crossmembers, the muffler under the cab floor between two crossmembers, in towards the middle over the rear axle (clear of the coil spring and the damper, under the load floor), back out under the two rear crossmembers, and out under the rear bar pointing back. It ran at the axle's height, through the top of the axle tube and through three crossmembers, and the muffler ran into a crossmember.
- **Rear wheel openings (item 3).** They went straight through the wheelhouse into the cabin, so the tan rear seat showed through their tops. Each rear wheelhouse has a dark inner wall now, 0.52 to 0.54 m out, clear of the coil spring and the tyre, and its top is 4 cm above the flare's opening (it was 5 mm below it). The rear seat is 1.02 m wide, between the wheelhouses (it was 1.44 m), and the load trims start above the wheelhouses.

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.** The winch's control box sits on the bar pan's top. The exhaust hangs inside the left rail: the muffler from a rubber hanger to the cab floor, the run over the axle from one to the load floor; its tail points back under the bar. Each rear wheelhouse wall stands on the body's inner side, facing the wheel. Neither spare moved: one stands upright in the load bay on the left, strapped to the side, facing across the car; the other lies flat on the rack, face up.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 50 pictures and nothing else: the standard set, the 28 audit pictures and 14 close-ups (the front from ahead and a corner, the bar from above and low, the winch box, the rear openings from each side and low, the exhaust from low, ahead, behind and the side, the rear seat and the load bay). It found the wheels, both spares, the rack and its load, the bar and lamps, the mirrors, aerial, mud flaps and recovery points attached, and nothing below the ground. What it found, and what was done (all there before this version):

- **An orange bar crossed the windscreen from outside and stuck out past both A-pillars.** It was the dashboard's padded top, 11 cm forward of the leaning glass and 1.66 m wide; the steel panel under it reached past the glass too. Both are 1.5 m wide and behind the glass, and the glovebox, radio and heater slides moved back onto the panel's new face.
- **The sky showed through each rear corner from inside, as a narrow slot.** The rounded corner panels show only from outside. Each band of each corner has an inside face now, black at the glass and trim below it.
- **Dark wedges hung below the body at each end of the rear openings.** The wheelhouses' bottoms ran 4 to 6 cm below the flares' feet; they stop at them now.
- **A thin tan line in the back of each rear opening.** The load floor's wide part started at the wheelhouse's back edge, where its front edge showed through; it starts 5 cm further back, and the narrow part runs on to meet it.
- **A slit in the rear floor under the seat's front edge, and the underside of the load floor in the carpet's tan.** A step closes the cab floor to the raised load floor, and a dark pan lies under the load floor.
- **The exhaust seemed to meet the rear axle.** A picture from directly under the axle shows the pipe over it, 0.26 m from the differential; this version's route had already taken it off the axle's line.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` and the Range Rover's tests pass.

## v2.17: the Telegraph Track North, stage 1 (the level, playable end to end)

The owner asked to continue building new tracks. The next one in SPEC.md §2 is the northern half of the Old Telegraph Track; it is built as the game's third episode, and its plan is `docs/PLAN-telegraph-north.md`, which also records, as built, every number below and every place the engine could not do what the design asked. The owner has not played it: this stage was built from the design and scripted drives alone. No vehicle body changed.

**The episode.** Episode 3, `telegraphNorth`, on its own map (`src/data/episodes/telegraphNorth.ts`, `telegraphNorthMap.ts`, `telegraphNorthRoute.ts`, `telegraphNorthWeather.ts`): the Bamaga Road turn-off to Nolans Brook, 1,929 m of route on a 1024 × 2048 m map, with one camp at Cannibal Creek, 59% of the way along. Load: 16 L, no jerry cans, one spare, two plugs, at `fuelScale` 12. Four briefing paragraphs. Notes at every place, with history kept to one source (Hema Maps on the northern track), recorded in `docs/HISTORY.md`. Timed sections: Canal Creek and Nolans Brook. What the two Telegraph maps share (the sand and bank track styles, the ledge, the winch rock, the surviving poles and the woodland grid) moved to `telegraphKit.ts`; the southern map is unchanged by it (`tests/telegraph.test.ts` passes).

**Why it is about water.** The southern episode is steep banks, and a second one of those would be more of the same. Hema describes the northern track by its water: a deep crossing with a right and a wrong way out, a log bridge, and Nolans Brook, up to 1.5 m deep, where the line decides whether a car makes it. So each wrong line here fails with water or a hole rather than a climb, and the deadline is the water itself.

**The mission: cross Nolans Brook before the rain lifts it.** The run starts fine at 1:30 pm. Showers come from 3 pm and steady rain from 4 pm (`telegraphNorthWeather.ts`); Nolans Brook takes the whole rise and the five creeks before it about a third (`shares`). The day's roll sets where the creeks start (0.1 m down when dry, 0.15 m up when wet) and scales the rain's rise. On the just-reopened day the brook is 0.10 m up at 4 pm, 0.26 m at 5 pm and 0.42 m at dark. Driven on the shoal at a crawl, the Hilux (the lowest intake) floods from a rise of about 0.25 m: from about 5 pm on that day (30 minutes of play), from about 3:45 pm on the wet day (19 minutes), never on the dry day. The LC79, with its snorkel, crosses until the wet day's 6 pm, when the current carries it off the shoal. Dark at 6:15 pm with an hour's grace is still the last deadline. The scripted clean run reaches Nolans at about 3:20 pm.

**The obstacles.** Canal Creek (a 13° ramp into water, a shelf 0.6 m under with over a metre either side, a winding exit to the left with a 15° pinch leaning 7°, and the old exit to the right: 3 m up in 8 m with two sheer gullies 0.9 m deep right across it). Sam and Cannibal Creeks (easy: 0.3 m, banks of 10 to 14°). Mistake Creek (4 m down in 7 m of clay with a rut, a 0.35 m ledge, lean one way then the other, and a washed-out hole 0.95 m deep on the right). Cypress Creek's log bridge (26 m of deck 4 m wide with no kerb; holes on the right, on the left and down the middle, each side hole leaving at least 2.45 m, for the Unimog, and at least 7.5 m of whole deck between them, for a long wheelbase). The long drive (372 m of sand with whoops, and a bog 0.6 m deep on black soil across the right of the track). Nolans Brook (40 m wide, 1.4 m in the middle and 2.3 to 2.4 m in two holes on the straight line, a 1.4 m/s current, and a shoal 0.55 m under on the upstream side).

**Why the way out of Nolans Brook is upstream.** The first line came back left to an exit downstream of the shoal. The game counts a car as carried off when the water lifts 45% of its weight and it moves downstream faster than 0.5 m/s for 4 s, and a car driving that leg at a crawl on a wet day did both: the Hilux reached 3.9 s on the right line. The line now goes in, upstream onto the shoal and straight across, so a car on it never drives downstream in deep water.

**Driven and passed** (`tests/telegraphNorthDrive.test.ts`, scripted drives with realistic damage, 29 tests): every obstacle at a crawl in low range with both lockers at 18 psi, in the short 40, the LC79 and the Hilux on the dry day; the Troop Carrier and the Unimog over the bridge; Nolans Brook on time on all three days in the Hilux, with the current never holding the car for 2 s; the long drive at 18 psi and not at 36. The wrong lines: both test cars held in the old exit's gullies; hung past 45° in Mistake Creek's hole; a wheel through the bridge down the middle and when held left past the first hole; bogged in the middle of the bog in high range; the 40 drowned and the LC79 stopped straight across Nolans; the Hilux drowned on the shoal at 5:30 pm where the LC79 crosses; the LC79 carried off at dark on the wet day. And the whole route end to end in the short 40 on 11.1 of its 16 L, in 17.3 minutes at the autopilot's crawl through each obstacle. `tests/telegraphNorth.test.ts` checks the layout and the episode's facts (13 tests). The test world is made ready for the storm as the game does (`prepareFlood`), without which a creek's rise does nothing; the southern map's test world is not, so its drive tests have never applied its dry and wet creek levels.

**Not done, and why.** The engine has one surface for every creek bed under water, so the shoal is shallower but not firmer, as Hema has it; a soft bed under water is an engine change (stage 2). The Unimog wades the straight line at Nolans. Mistake Creek's way in does not punish speed on the right line. The bridge is drawn as sawn boards, not logs. Fruit Bat, Eliot and Twin Falls, traffic, a drowned traveller to recover and a fallen tree are stage 2. The eight vehicles not named above were not driven.

**Looked at.** Screenshots from the free camera at Canal Creek, Mistake Creek, the bridge, the bog and Nolans Brook, and the journey page with three episodes at desktop and phone sizes. Canal's two exits, Mistake's chute, the bridge and the bog read clearly. At Nolans the water is only a little paler over the shoal and darker over the holes; the sign and the note say "hard right". No tree showed by the bog in the picture taken from the track. Both are in the README's checklist for the play test.

**Browser tests.** `welcome:4` and `launch:40` now expect three episodes.

## v2.18: the Camel Trophy Ninety

The owner asked to continue updating the vehicles; this is the next of those not yet reviewed. Only the Camel Trophy Land Rover Ninety (`camel`) changes. Its folder has no photographs, and its `SOURCES.md` sends the body's shape to the Perentie's folder. So its 28 audit pictures and the standard set were compared with sheet B of `reference/vehicles/land-rover-110-perentie/drawing-variants.png`, which is a Land Rover 90 hardtop from the side and the rear, measured at 310 pixels a metre from its 2.36 m wheelbase; with `side-right-county-1990.jpg`, `front-34-county.jpg` and `soft-top-british-110.jpg` for the wings, the wheel openings and the step at the waist; and with the Perentie's own fixes since v1.91. The equipment was checked against the Camel Trophy Owners Club account quoted in `SOURCES.md`. There is still no photograph of a 1986 car, so the cage, the rack and the plaques are checked against that account only. The list is in `docs/AUDIT-2026-11.md`, and the measurements are in a new table in the vehicle's `SOURCES.md`.

- **Where the body sits (item 1).** The body is 0.12 m further back on its wheels: the wing's front is 0.505 m ahead of the front axle and the tail 0.64 m behind the rear one (the drawing has 0.50 m, and 0.64 m to the body's rear face). They were 0.625 and 0.58, so the front wheels sat back under a long nose. The body is drawn in its own frame and moved back as one piece (`BODY_SHIFT` in `src/render/camelBody.ts`). The sills are 5 cm higher (0.12 m). On the body itself the bonnet is 0.07 m longer, the front doors 0.07 m shorter and the rear body 0.06 m shorter, as the drawing has them. The collision boxes, the intake, the winch's rope point, the tow point, the headlamp and roof-lamp beams and the driver's seat, wheel and dials moved with the body.
- **Cab (item 2).** Above the waist the cab is 1.53 m wide, stepped in 7 cm a side from the 1.67 m body, with a ledge along the waist from the windscreen to the tail. It was 1.67 m wide to the gutters. The dash, the dog guard and the rear seat are narrower to fit.
- **Wheel openings (item 3).** Flat-topped with radiused corners and splayed sides under a black eyebrow, to the Perentie's figures (the same wings and tyres): the top is 0.17 m over the tyre, because a crossed axle lifts a wheel 0.16 m. They were half circles under a thick lip.
- **Windscreen, doors and roof (item 4).** The windscreen leans back 18 degrees (it was 7), and each front door's leading edge and glass lean with it. The doors and windscreen end at 1.36 m and the roof above them is 0.17 m deep: a band over the gutter, a chamfer with the Alpine window in it, and a flat top 1.2 m wide. The doors reached 1.42 m and the roof was 0.11 m deep.
- **Raised intake (item 5).** It enters a box on the front corner of the left wing top, lies along the wing top under two straps, and climbs beside the left windscreen pillar, outboard of the cage's leg and 8 to 11 cm ahead of it, to a head at roof level, as the account describes. It lay in the air beside the wing and climbed 0.16 m out from the cab.
- **Cage (item 6).** Each leg stands on a foot bolted to the body side, upright to the waist, then steps in with the cab; the A hoop leans back along the windscreen pillars. The B hoop's feet are behind the front doors, 3 cm clear of their rear edges; they stood on the doors. The two bars joining the hoops over the roof are 0.62 m out.
- **Exhaust (item 7).** On the left, inside the chassis rail: out of the back of the engine, down ahead of the cab floor, under the cab's two crossmembers with the silencer between them, up over the rear axle under the load floor (0.29 m over the axle tube at rest, 0.14 m at full bump), down again, and out under the rear crossmember pointing back. It began in mid-air, ran through the top of the axle tube and ended turned out behind the left rear wheel.
- **Winch (item 8).** Its drum is a plain drum between two flanges; it was a stack of rings. It stays on the bumper in front of the grille: the account gives a Warn 8274, an upright winch that sits on the bumper, inside a Brownchurch bull bar with a hawse fairlead. The fairlead is on the cradle's front face.
- **Wheel boxes (item 9).** Each has an inner wall, a roof and a wall across each end, and a dark liner round the opening's inner edge. The rear box reaches in past the coil spring to the chassis rail below the load floor; its wall had cut through the spring. Inside the cab each rear wheel box is a Sandglow box over the dark one.
- **Damper brackets (item 10).** A drop plate from each chassis rail carries the feet of the shared suspension's damper braces; at the front, where the rails are 0.3 m out, an outrigger carries the plate. The feet stood in the air. The shared code is not changed.
- **Footwells (item 11).** The bulkhead under the dash reaches the floor.
- **Rear lamp guards (item 12).** Each guard's four corners turn back onto the corner panel.
- **Rack (item 13).** 1.44 m wide to suit the narrower cab (it was 1.52 m) and 2.03 m long (it was 2.29 m; the roof is 0.22 m shorter now that the windscreen leans), on eight feet clamped to the gutters, which stand between the window bars and clear of the cage's hoops. The roof plaque's bracket stops behind the plaque.
- **Underside (item 14).** The back of the step under the load floor is dark.

**Where each part is attached and which way it faces.** Positions are in the vehicle's frame (z back, axles at ±1.18 m). The bonnet spare lies flat on a 0.28 m block on the bonnet, face up, its hub on the centre line 0.03 m behind the front axle, under a clamp bar and its nut; with the body it is 0.16 m further back than before. The door spare hangs on a boss on the rear door, facing back, its hub 0.12 m right of centre and 0.255 m behind the door; it moved 0.06 m back with the tail. Both are placed with `mountSpare()`. The intake's box sits on the left wing top's front corner; its tube is strapped to the wing top twice and clamped twice to the A hoop's left leg; its head faces up at the roof's front left corner, 0.915 m out and 0.12 m ahead of the hoop's corner. The cage's four feet are bolted to the body sides, two ahead of the front doors and two behind them; a swivel lamp stands on an arm on each A leg, facing forward. The rack's eight feet clamp the roof gutters, with a leg from each to the lower rail; its four work lamps and the plaque stand on posts on its front rail, facing forward; the two jerry cans stand upright in cradles at its right rear; the canvas bag is strapped down at its left rear and the steel box sits ahead of that. Each sand ladder hangs outside a side rail on two of the rack's posts, facing out. The winch stands in its cradle on the bumper between the bull bar's hoop legs, drum across the car, gear case and motor on the car's left, fairlead facing forward. The exhaust hangs inside the left chassis rail: the silencer from a rubber hanger to the cab floor, the run over the axle from one to the load floor, the tail from a bracket on the rear crossmember; its tip points back, 0.25 m left of centre, 3.5 cm past the crossmember. The shovel is clamped to the roof's rear face above the door; the mirrors' arms stand on the front doors' upper front corners, below the waist; the rear lamp guards are on the corner panels.

**The second look.** A reviewer was given 89 pictures and nothing else: the standard set (8), the 28 audit pictures and 53 close-ups. A usage limit stopped it part-way; it was resumed and opened all 89. It found no floating parts, no wheel or spare edge-on or reversed, nothing touching or below the ground and no open gaps between the outside panels, and it found both spares, the exhaust, the rack and its load, the tools, the winch and the A-post lamps attached. It listed six places where it could not tell from the pictures whether parts were clear of each other. Each was measured on the model, and what was done:

- **The intake's cap ran into the corner of the cage's front hoop** (made by this version). The cap is 0.093 m in radius and its centre stood 0.093 m from the axis of the hoop's leg, whose tube is 0.027 m in radius: 2.7 cm inside it. The riser leaned more than the leg and met it at the top. It now leans a little less than the leg and stands 8 to 11 cm ahead of it all the way up, and the cap clears the hoop's leg and corner by 2.3 cm. The point the engine breathes at moved 9 cm forward with the head. Photographed again from the front left, the side, above, behind, ahead and low.
- **The left door mirror looked hard against the intake's riser.** Not touching: the mirror's face was 6 cm behind the riser, and is 8 cm behind it now that the riser stands further forward. From the driver's seat the mirror shows through the door glass, left of the door's window bar.
- **The cage's feet looked as if they sat across the door shut lines.** They do not: each front foot ends 2.5 cm ahead of the front door's leading edge, and each rear foot starts 3 cm behind its rear edge. The pictures were taken at an angle.
- **A grey pointed part above each tyre.** The shared suspension's shock tower, 0.47 m out; the tyre's inner face is 0.64 m out. Not changed.
- **The sand ladders on the rack's sides looked splayed out at the top.** They are built upright. The picture looks along two plates 1.9 m long from below their height, so the near end shows higher and further out than the far end. Not changed.
- **A short lever with a black knob between the driver's seat and the left door, joined to nothing.** The door's inside release, on each door's inner panel below the glass (there before). Not changed.
- Also noted and not changed: the tailpipe's tip looked turned inboard (it points straight back, 0.25 m left of centre); and a bright wedge above the rear seat's back in one picture from inside (the inside of the roof's chamfer catching the light; no sky shows through it).

The reviewer said 13 of the close-ups had only a short first look. Those 13, the six new pictures of the intake and four pictures of the final car were given to a second reviewer in a batch of 22. It opened all 22 and found nothing clearly broken: no floating part, no spare edge-on or reversed, nothing below the ground and no open gap between panels. It listed five places where it could not confirm clearance, and each was measured:

- **The intake's cap against the cage's bend, after the fix.** One of its four pictures shows the gap; the others look along it. Measured, the cap is 2.3 cm clear of the hoop's leg at the cap's height and 3 cm clear of the corner. Not changed again: the riser follows the pillar, and the two are clamped together below.
- **The riser and the hoop's leg side by side lower down.** Their surfaces are 5 cm apart, joined by the two clamps.
- **The bull bar's hoop leg against the winch's tall gear case.** Black on black in the pictures. The leg's inner surface is 0.343 m out and the gear case's outer face 0.305 m: 3.8 cm apart. Not changed.
- **The exhaust across the left rear coil spring.** The pipe is 0.25 m left of centre and the spring's inner edge about 0.44 m: 17 cm apart. The picture looks across them. Its zigzag is the route: under the cab's crossmembers, up over the axle, down under the rear crossmember. Not changed.
- **A grey rod with a rounded end hanging behind the front axle.** The exhaust's downpipe from the engine, seen from ahead, with its bend end-on. Not changed.
- Also noted and not changed: the shovel is held by a clip on its handle and a clamp over its blade, both on the roof's rear face; each A-post lamp stands on one arm from the cage's leg.

Each fix was photographed again and read, and the standard and audit sets were taken again on the final version. `tests/spares.test.ts` and `tests/camel.test.ts` pass; the latter has a new test that measures the wing's front and the tail from the axles and the widths of the body and the cab. Outside the vehicle's three files, the change touches only the camel's own entries in `src/data/vehicles.ts` (its cabin is 0.12 m further back) and `src/render/vehicleLooks.ts` (its headlamp and roof-lamp positions).

Not changed: the driver's view. The windscreen's header and the roof take the top quarter of it, as before; the eye is 0.12 m under the header, the same as in the Perentie, whose cabin this is. The shared axle tubes' ends look saw-toothed close up at the hubs; that is the shared suspension, the same on every vehicle.

70.2k triangles with wheels and boards (body 51.1k; it was 72.2k and 53.1k).

## v2.19: play test fixes (the winch chooser, Canal Creek's shelf, low range)

Codex play tested v2.18 on the Telegraph Track North in the FJ40 on Mild, in a desktop browser, as far as Canal Creek, and made three suggestions. All three are done. It asked to keep the rollover prompt and the trip journal; neither changed. It did not reach the long drive's bog or Nolans Brook, so whether those can be seen in time is still open (`docs/PLAN-telegraph-north.md`).

**1. The winch chooser shows where the pull will take you.** The chooser gave the anchor's kind and distance, but the selected anchor could be off the screen or behind the windscreen pillar, and the line to it was a one-pixel dashed line in the roof's colour.

- **An arrow for an anchor out of view** (`edgeArrow` in `src/core/anchorPicker.ts`). While choosing, if the selected anchor is behind the camera or within 4% of the view's edge or beyond it, an amber arrow stands on a frame 84% of the way out from the centre, towards the anchor, pointing at it, with the distance under it (the same distance the prompt gives, from the winch to the anchor). A point behind the camera projects mirrored through the centre, so its direction is turned back; dead behind points down. The arrow is a page element over the view, so it shows in every camera, and pulses unless the system asks for reduced motion.
- **A bright bar where the cable would run** (`src/render/tools.ts`). A bar 7 cm thick in amber, drawn over everything, from the winch to the selected anchor. The ring over the anchor was already drawn over everything, so it shows through a pillar.

**2. Canal Creek: where to put the wheels.** The sign and the journal made the left exit clear. From the cab the shelf showed only as a faint paler patch, and Codex drove off it into the flooding warning. Nothing about the crossing is easier: the shelf is as deep (0.6 m) and as wide as it was.

- **Two marker rocks at the near water's edge**, one either side of the shelf's line, 5.6 m apart: drive in between them.
- **A sign on the far bank straight across the shelf**, between the two exits, to aim at: "Aim here — then exit left". The approach sign reads "Canal Creek — between the rocks, exit left". A sketch on the sign was the other suggestion; the game's signs carry one line of text and no drawing.
- **Darker water beside the shelf.** The two holes either side moved in to start at the shelf's edges, so the water beside it is 1.7 m deep (it was 1.1 m for the first few metres, then 1.6 m) and the paler shelf has a dark edge on both sides.
- The note and the route hint say the same: low range, crawl, in between the rocks, aim at the sign, then left.

**3. Onboarding.**

- **The two buttons.** The journey page's button reads **Load episode** (it read Drive, like the briefing's button after it); for a saved camp it still reads Continue.
- **Low range before the first water** (`src/core/lowRangeHint.ts`). With water 0.2 m or deeper within 30 m ahead of the car, along its heading, while it is in high range, a prompt reads "Water ahead: stop, then low range" with the range key (on a phone, "stop, then tap 4WD and LOW"). It teaches once: after low range has been used on a run it does not show again, so an easy ford can be taken in high range without being told. The first version used the map's stored distance to a creek, which only reaches as far as the creek's carved valley (about 6 m at Canal Creek); looking for water along the heading shows the prompt 29 m out and not when facing away. It applies on every episode; on the CREB the orientation stretch teaches low range before the first water.

**Looked at.** Canal Creek from the driver's eye height on the ramp, before and after: the rocks frame the way in, the shelf's edges are darker, and the sign shows over the far bank. In the game in a browser: the journey button reads Load episode; the prompt shows at 29 m and 6 m from Canal Creek's water and not at 48 m or facing away; the chooser's bar runs to the selected rock; with the anchors behind the car the arrow shows at the bottom of the view with the distance, and with them ahead it does not. No vehicle body changed.

**Tests.** `tests/anchorArrow.test.ts` (where the arrow goes), `tests/lowRangeHint.test.ts` (when the prompt shows, and that it sees Canal Creek from the top of its ramp), and Canal Creek's rocks, sign and depths in `tests/telegraphNorth.test.ts`. The scripted drives of the northern track pass unchanged. `launch:116` expects the button to read Load episode.

## v2.20: the two sightline checks the play test did not reach (the long drive's bog, Nolans Brook)

Codex's play test of v2.18 stopped at Canal Creek, so two checks from stage 1 stayed open: can the long drive's bog and the shoal at Nolans Brook be seen from the seat, and is there something to winch to at the bog. Both were checked here in the game in a browser, from the chase camera and the driver's seat, and both needed a change. Nothing about either obstacle is easier to drive: no depth, width, surface or current changed.

**The bog had nothing to winch to.** From 35 m and from 18 m the bog shows as a dark patch across the right of the pale sand, with a paler strip left of it: it can be seen in time. But the bog asked for a tree at either end (`winchTrees`), and none was there. The engine plants winch trees only at the mud holes it makes itself in rough tracks (`features.mudHoles`), not at a bog a map lays by hand (`map.bogs`), so the flag did nothing; the CREB's hand-laid bogs have rocks placed beside them for the same reason. Measured from the bog's middle and both ends, there was no anchor rock within the cable's 29 m and one tree at 28 m from one end.

- **Two rocks to winch to** (`LONG_DRIVE_BOG_ANCHORS` in `telegraphNorthRoute.ts`): one 17 m before the bog's middle and one 17 m past it, 3.6 m left of the centreline, on the firm side, marked with a ring like the map's other anchor rocks. From either end of the bog one is ahead and one behind, both within reach. Pulling to the one ahead draws the car forward and left, onto the firm edge.
- The bog's `winchTrees` flag is removed, since it did nothing. Making the engine honour it at hand-laid bogs is still on the roadmap (it would also plant trees along the CREB).
- The route hint and the note say there is a rock at each end.

**Nolans Brook's shoal could not be told from the deep.** From above the shoal is a paler strip with dark holes beside it. From the bank, 2 m above the water, the whole brook is one grey-green, in the chase camera and from the seat. The sign and the note said "hard right", which does not say how far right or where to turn across. As at Canal Creek (v2.19), the line is now marked and the water is untouched:

- **A rock on the near bank at the water's edge**, at x = 18, the middle of the shoal's run across, 0.7 m in radius and not an anchor, with **a sign above it** facing back along the bank: "Turn across here".
- **A sign on the far bank beside the way out**, straight across from the rock: "Aim here from the rock". The way out's pale sand ramp already showed from the near bank; the sign stands 2.8 m right of it.
- The approach sign reads "Nolans Brook — right to the rock, then across". The note and the route hint say the same: in, hard right, along the bank in the shallows to the rock, then turn and drive straight at the sign.
- The rock stands 0.3 m above the brook's level on the commonest day and 1.5 m back from the water. At dark on the wettest day (0.73 m up) the water is round the rock; its top still shows, and the sign above it stands on dry ground.

**The line by the marks is a second right line.** The track's own line cuts the corner in a curve; the marks describe a squarer one (along the bank about 7 m out from the water's edge, then straight across at x = 18). Both are on the shoal all the way, 0.55 m deep. A new scripted drive takes the marked line in the short 40 on the commonest day and in the Hilux on the wettest (at the day's start): both cross with a dry engine and are never carried.

**Not changed.** The warning "Deep, fast water — the current is lifting the car" shows on the shoal, on the right line. It is true there (the current pushes the car towards the deep), and the note says to hold the car against it. Whether the rise is too fast or too slow is still for the owner's play test.

**Looked at** (in the game in a browser, low graphics, the short 40). The bog from 35 m and 18 m back, before the change; the bog from above with both rocks, and the far rock from beside the track: each rock sits on the ground beside the left edge of the track, clear of it, ring uppermost. Nolans Brook from the way in, from the turn, from along the bank and from the rock, in the chase camera and from the seat; the rock and its sign close up (the sign faces the car coming along the bank; the rock sits on the sand at the water's edge); the far sign close up (on the bank right of the way out, above the water, with the two anchor rocks beyond it). With the note's card open, the card covers the near sign from the chase camera on the way along the bank; the rock shows below it, and the card folds away by itself. No vehicle body changed, so no studio renders and no second look by a reviewer.

**Tests.** `tests/telegraphNorth.test.ts`: the bog's two rocks (17 m either side of its middle, off the track, on the side away from the bog, in reach from both ends), and the Nolans marks (the rock dry and within 2 m of the water, the turn sign behind and above it facing west, the far sign dry and beside the way out, and the shoal no deeper than 0.7 m all the way from the way in to the rock and from the rock across). `tests/telegraphNorthDrive.test.ts`: the marked line driven (30 drives now).

**The browser tests, run in full on v2.19** (65 tests, 1.3 hours): 56 passed and 9 failed. Five are on the roadmap's list of long-standing failures (`episode:15`, `m4-tools:15`, `night:4`, `touch:234`, and the flaky `vehicles:13`). The other four were run again on v2.16, the version before this session's work, to see whether they are new. `sideTrips:4` fails there too (the test expects three side trips in the journal and the CREB has four). `toolMenu:4` is flaky: it passed on v2.16, and alone on this build it passed twice and failed once. `touch:82` and `uiSize:71` were still running on v2.16 when this shipped; they finished afterwards and fail there the same way, so none of the four is new. The roadmap's list carries all four.

## v2.21: the Telegraph Track North, stage 2 (the falls, other travellers, a tree down)

The owner asked to continue the northern track with stage 2. Everything on the plan's list is built except the soft bed under water, which is an engine change and comes next. Built without a play test. The plan and its as-built figures are in `docs/PLAN-telegraph-north.md`; the sources are in `docs/HISTORY.md`.

**Sources first.** Hema's guide to the northern track was read again for the falls. It says: a fork to the right off the start of the track goes to Fruit Bat Falls, day access only, a wide blue-green pool and cascade; the turn-off for Eliot, Twin and Indian Head Falls is just before Canal Creek, with several falls, swimming areas and plenty of camping; most traffic runs south to north with few places to pass; helping bogged or waterlogged travellers is worthwhile. The Queensland Parks page refused the request, so nothing comes from it. Hema names no creek for any of the falls, so the game names none.

**The falls.** Two side tracks, both to the right before Canal Creek, as Hema places them.

- **Fruit Bat Falls** (`fruit-bat-track`): the fork is 56 m from the turn-off, with a sign. 210 m east over the heath to a stop on the bank 3.2 m above the pool. A creek of the game's own comes in from the south, east of the track, drops 2.2 m over a lip 24 m wide into a pool 26 m wide, and leaves the map to the east without crossing the track.
- **Eliot and Twin Falls** (`eliot-track`): the turn-off is 17 m before the top of Canal Creek's ramp, with a sign. About 165 m east along the creek's south bank to a camping area (a fire ring, two log seats). Canal Creek now drops twice upstream of the crossing, 1.1 m (Eliot Falls) and 1.15 m (Twin Falls), with the camping area between them. The creek's level at the crossing, its shelf and its holes are unchanged; the scripted drives of Canal Creek pass as before.
- **One side trip, "The falls"**: stop three seconds at each, in either order; a traveller hands over one tyre plug when both are done. The trip is in the journal with directions.
- **What it costs.** The mission is a race against the rain, so the falls are paid for in time. The autopilot takes 147 s out and back for Fruit Bat and 113 s for Eliot and Twin; the whole route with both took 8.3 minutes longer than without (about an hour on the game's clock) and burnt 13.8 L of the 16 L against 11.1 L. At that pace Nolans Brook is reached at about 4:20 pm: still a wade on the commonest day, and too deep for the Hilux on the wettest. The load is unchanged: both falls fit in the tank, with 2 L to spare.
- **The camping area is not a camp:** the run is not saved there. One camp, at Cannibal Creek, is the episode's design.
- **The low-range prompt** (v2.19) showed at Fruit Bat's stop, because the pool is water ahead. It no longer shows while a side trip's stop is on offer (`src/core/game.ts`).

**Other travellers.**

- **A Patrol coming south** along the sand between Sam Creek and Mistake Creek (192 m), the first time either end is reached. It keeps to the track's line and waits while the car is on it ahead: pull off to let it by. The briefing says so.
- **A drowned HiLux in Nolans Brook** (`nolans-drowned`), in the old straight line across, 9 m left of the track's line where it turns onto the shoal, nose towards the shoal, in 1.26 m of water on the commonest day: over the bonnet, with the cab and the rack showing. It is within 20 m of the shoal's start and of the near bank, so the winch reaches it from either. Pulled 6 m it is free, standing in under 0.75 m, and its driver hands over a jerry can. Winching it out takes time while the brook rises; the jerry can counts in the score's fuel share. It also marks the wrong line: a car that goes straight across meets it. It was first placed 11 m off in 1.34 m, where only the roof showed; at 9 m the bonnet is at the surface.
- **A dead tree down across the long drive**, one a run, between 50 and 205 m along, at a spot the run's seed picks, at least 24 m from the bog. On open heath a car can go round it; in the southern episode's gallery forest it cannot.

**Not built: a soft bed under water.** Every creek bed under water is one surface, so Nolans Brook's sandy middle is no softer than its shoal. This is the last item of stage 2.

**Looked at** (in the game in a browser, low graphics, the short 40). The fork to Fruit Bat Falls from the track (the side track and its sign show to the right); the stop above the pool in the chase camera; the falls from beside the pool and from above (a wide pale step with water over it into a dark pool; the first version dropped over 9 m and read as a ramp, the second over 3.5 m and read as a weir with dry rock, the third over 5.5 m with deeper water on the lip); Eliot Falls and Twin Falls from the bank (two short rapids with white water); the camping area from above; the drowned HiLux from the way in and close up, at both positions; the Patrol coming down the track 14 s after reaching its end. The side trip completes at both stops ("The falls complete · A tyre plug from a traveller at the falls"). Not looked at: the tree coming down (the planner's choice of spot is tested for twelve seeds), and winching the HiLux out by hand. No vehicle body changed, so no studio renders and no second look by a reviewer.

**Tests.** `tests/telegraphNorthStage2.test.ts` (7): where each side track leaves the road and ends, that both are easy and dry; Fruit Bat's drop, lip, pool and stop; Eliot and Twin Falls' drops, the camp between them, and the crossing's level and shelf unchanged; places, notes, signs and the side trip, which completes in either order and is never offered from the main track; the traffic's track; the HiLux's depth, distance from the right line and reach from the shoal; one tree a run for twelve seeds, clear of the bog and the water, on more than one stretch. `tests/telegraphNorthDrive.test.ts` (33 drives): each side track out and back in high range, and the whole route with both falls on the fuel carried. The autopilot counts a path done within 2.5 m of its last point, so an out-and-back path that ends where it starts finishes at once; the two short drives end 6 m off the track instead.

## v2.22: a soft bed under Nolans Brook (the last of stage 2)

Hema says Nolans Brook has a soft sandy bottom and that the hard right line is usually shallower with a firmer bottom. Until now the game's shoal was only shallower: every creek bed under water was one firm surface. This update adds a soft bed a creek can ask for, and Nolans Brook asks for it.

- **A new surface, soft sand under water** (`SurfaceId.SoftBed` in `src/data/surfaces.ts`): grip 0.42 (a firm bed has 0.6), rolling resistance 1.4 and 0.2 m of sink. Low tyre pressures cut its sink and drag as they do on dry sand. It sounds and washes the car like any creek bed.
- **A creek asks for it** (`CreekDef.softBed`). The bed is then soft everywhere under water except on the creek's bars and where a track fords it (within half the track's width and half a metre). No other creek on any map sets it, so nothing else changes: the southern episode's sand creeks are as they were.
- **At Nolans Brook** the shoal (its two bars) and the track's line are firm; the old straight line, the deep middle and the water upstream of the shoal are soft. Both right lines, the track's curve and the marked line of v2.20, are on firm bed wheel to wheel.

**Why the resistance is so high.** The first values copied dry sand (resistance 0.13, then 0.28, then 0.62) and a crawling LC79 crossed the soft bed as if it were not there. Rolling resistance in this engine grows with speed up to 0.6 m/s, so it sets a creeping speed and never stops a car outright; and in 1.35 m of water the car is half afloat, so its wheels carry half their load. A probe (LC79, low range, open diffs, 18 psi) crept at 0.14 m/s at 1.6. At 1.4 the scripted LC79 with both lockers at 18 psi crosses 25 m of soft bed in about 78 s (0.32 m/s, against 0.7 m/s on the shoal), and at road pressures (36 psi) does about 0.09 m/s and is not across in 250 s.

**What it does to the wrong lines.**

- **Just off the shoal** (9 m downstream of the right line, in 1.35 m): a snorkelled car that could wade this before now crawls at under half speed aired down, and all but stops at road pressures, with the brook rising. A car with no snorkel floods there, as before.
- **In the game**, the short 40 put on the soft bed in high range at full throttle made no headway in 6 s and was carried 2.7 m downstream; on the shoal beside it the same 6 s took it up to 2 m/s.
- **The old straight line**: the short 40 now stops in the middle's 1.35 m, short of the dug-out holes (it reached them before). The LC79 still stops in the holes.
- **The Unimog still wades the straight line** (144 s for the scripted run). Its intake is 2.3 m up and it weighs 6 t. Not changed.

**The way in was soft under the left wheel.** The track enters the water at the edge of the shoal's first bar, and the left wheel was 9 cm outside it. A bar's firm top was first taken as its ellipse plus 5%; the fix was the rule above, that a track's ford is firm. A test checks the track's line wheel to wheel wherever it is in the water.

**Looked at.** The game in a browser, the short 40 at Nolans Brook: on the shoal and on the soft bed beside it, six seconds of full throttle each (above). The bed looks as it did; only what the wheels meet has changed. No vehicle body changed, so no studio renders and no second look by a reviewer.

**Tests.** `tests/telegraphNorthStage2.test.ts` (10 now): the surface's figures against sand and firm bed; the shoal and both right lines firm, the rest of the brook soft, the drowned HiLux on soft bed; no other creek soft, and every soft cell on the map within Nolans Brook's reach. `tests/telegraphNorthDrive.test.ts` (34 drives): the LC79 off the shoal at 18 psi (over half as long again as the right line, on a path two thirds as long) and at 36 psi (not across in 250 s). The straight-line drive's depth figure for the short 40 changed from over 1.4 m to over 1.3 m, since the soft bed now stops it before the holes.

**The full unit suite on v2.21** was run on a clean copy of that version while this was being built: 118 files, 1,099 tests passed and 2 to do.

## v2.23: play test fixes (the starting load, Nolans Brook's turn, the falls' trade-off)

Codex spot-tested v2.22 on the Telegraph Track North in the FJ40 on Mild, in a fresh browser with no save. It did not clear the long drive's bog, cross Nolans Brook or try the HiLux recovery, so those are still not play tested. It reported that the Load episode and Drive buttons, Canal Creek's line, the winch arrow and choosing an end of the fallen tree all worked. Three things to fix, done here.

**1. The starting load was another episode's.** With no save, choosing Episode 3 gave 50 L, a jerry can, two spares and three plugs: the CREB's load. **Packing → Standard load** gave the 16 L, no can, one spare and two plugs the episode is designed round. A first-time player got an easier run without choosing it, and the fuel and kit share of the score was measured against the wrong load.

- **Cause.** The load was remembered per vehicle, not per episode, and every run stored its load there, the standard one included. The journey page took its starting load from the episode it opened on (the CREB) and kept it when another episode was picked.
- **Fix** (`src/data/packing.ts`: `packFor`, `rememberPack`, `isStandardPack`). A load is remembered per episode and vehicle, and only when it was packed by hand, meaning it differs from that episode's standard load. Picking an episode or a vehicle on the journey page shows that episode's load: the hand-packed one for it if there is one, else its standard load. **Standard load** forgets the hand-packed one.
- **Old saves.** A save from before this update holds one load per vehicle with no record of which episode it was for. It is kept for the episode last played, and only if it is not that episode's standard load; otherwise it is dropped. A load packed by hand for one episode therefore no longer follows the vehicle to another.

**2. Nolans Brook: the turn.** The drowned HiLux was easy to see; the rock and sign that mark the turn were not, and Codex turned right early, onto the grassy bank, before the water. The HUD said "Right along the bank to the rock"; only the history card said to go in first.

- **The HUD says the sequence:** "Into the water, hard right to the big rock, then straight across to the sign. Crawl". The approach sign reads "Nolans Brook — in, right to the big rock, then across", and the note says to drive into the water first.
- **The marker is a big rock:** 1.3 m in radius (it was 0.7 m), at the same spot at the near water's edge, with its sign behind it. From the turn in the water it now stands out on the sand.

**3. The falls: the trade-off before the fork.** The journal explained that both stops earn one tyre plug and that the delay lets Nolans Brook rise, but nothing said so on the track. The first stretch (the turn-off road) now has a HUD hint, which shows from the start and through both forks: "Forks to the right go to the falls: a tyre plug for both, but Nolans Brook rises meanwhile".

**Looked at** (in the game in a browser, low graphics, the short 40). The first stretch 20 m before the Fruit Bat fork: the hint is under the track's name with the fork and its sign ahead. Nolans Brook from the way in (the new hint; the HiLux ahead) and from the turn in the water (the big rock on the bank to the right, its sign behind it). The fuel gauge reads 16 L on a new run of Episode 3. No vehicle body changed, so no studio renders and no second look by a reviewer.

**Tests.** `tests/packing.test.ts` (4 new): a new player's load on each episode; a hand-packed load kept for its episode and vehicle and nowhere else; the standard load not remembered, and a hand-packed one forgotten when put back; old saves read as above. `e2e/launch.spec.ts` (1 new, `launch:73`): in a fresh browser the journey page shows 50 L on the CREB and 16 L on both Telegraph episodes without opening Packing, an extra plug packed for the northern track stays there, and Standard load puts it back. The launch and welcome browser tests pass (10). The tests below the new one in `launch.spec.ts` moved down 29 lines.

**Not run: the full suites on v2.22.** The container restarted twice and stopped them both times. They run on this version after the merge.

## v2.24: recovery that is easier to read (the note makes way, a cue back to the line, named boulders)

Codex tested v2.23 with targeted drives and recoveries in a live browser and a phone emulation of the journey menu. Its verdict: keep the difficulty, and make recovery easier to read. The right line at Nolans Brook finished undamaged in low range; the straight line at 5:30 pm flooded the engine; the HiLux recovery paid its jerry can and dragged the FJ40 about 6 m sideways into the deep-water warning; the bog's forward boulder pulled the car about 15 m onto the firm edge; both falls registered and paid one plug; the starting load is right and custom loads stay with their episode. It did not do one uninterrupted run, or test on a physical phone or controller.

**Nothing is easier to drive.** No depth, surface, current, load or score changed. Three changes to what the HUD says during and after a recovery.

**1. The history note makes way** (`HistoryCard.makeWay` in `src/ui/episodeCards.ts`). In a short desktop window the note overlapped the winch prompt while the flood warning was up. When a warning (any prompt marked "!"), the winch chooser or a recovery starts, a note being read while driving shrinks to its title and first lines, the form it already takes once the car drives on, and closes seven seconds later. It happens once as the warning starts, so a note opened again by hand stays open. A note read with the game stopped is left alone. The trip journal keeps the whole note, as before.

**2. A cue back to the line after a recovery** (`src/core/recoveryCue.ts`). A track's hint shows only within about a metre of its edge, so after a winch pull or righting the car, metres off the track and facing another way, the HUD said nothing about where to go. Now, from the moment the winch comes off or the car is back on its wheels, for 45 seconds or until the car is on the line again, the hint reads "Back to Nolans Brook" with "The line is 8 m to your right." and then the track's own hint. The distance and side are to the nearest point of the nearest track that has a hint, within 40 m, and update as the car moves; the side is ahead, behind, left or right of the way the car faces. Once the car is back on the line the ordinary hint takes over and the cue does not return until the next recovery. It applies on every episode.

**3. The bog's boulders have names.** Both were "Boulder" in the winch chooser. A map can now name an anchor rock (`BoulderDef.label`); the long drive's are **Approach boulder** (before the bog, going north) and **Exit boulder** (past it). Every other marked rock is still "Boulder".

**Not changed: the falls.** Codex noted that the extra plug does not raise an undamaged run's kit score, and that the full route with both detours uses 13.8 of the 16 L, and recommended keeping the falls an exploration choice and not something a good score needs. That is how they are: the plug only counts for a run that has used one, and both stops cost time against the rising brook. Left as they are.

**Looked at** (the game in a browser at 1280 × 600, low graphics, the short 40, at Nolans Brook). With the brook's note open in full, opening the winch chooser: the note shrinks to three lines in the top right and clears the prompts in the bottom right, and the chooser names the drowned HiLux's front recovery point with its distance. After attaching, detaching and stopping 8 m downstream of the line facing away from the crossing: "Back to Nolans Brook. The line is 8 m to your right. Into the water, hard right to the big rock, then straight across to the sign. Crawl", above the dials, with the deep-water warning in the prompts and the note folded. No vehicle body changed, so no studio renders and no second look by a reviewer.

**Tests.** `tests/recoveryCue.test.ts` (4): when the cue starts, how long it lasts and what ends it; the words for ahead, behind, left and right; that the nearest hinted track is found from off the track only while the cue is up, and that 6 m downstream of the Nolans line it is to the right; the two named boulders, the approach one south of the bog and the exit one north. `e2e/recovery.spec.ts` (1): in the bog with the long drive's note open, the chooser shrinks the note and lists both boulders by name; after detaching 8 m west of the track the hint reads "Back to The long drive" with "The line is … m to your right"; back on the track the ordinary hint returns, and driving off it again shows no cue. A tree is now among the anchors in reach of the bog in the game (the falls' creek changed where trees grow), so the browser test steps through the anchors.

**The full suites on v2.23.** Unit: 118 files, 1,107 passed and 2 to do. Browser: 66 tests, 58 passed and 8 failed, all on the roadmap's list (`episode:15`, `m4-tools:15`, `night:4`, `sideTrips:4`, `toolMenu:4`, `touch:82`, `touch:234`, `uiSize:71`). `vehicles:13`, which is flaky, passed, and so did the new `launch:73`.

## v2.25: Canal Creek can be read from the ramp (clear water, a pale shelf, worn wheel lines)

The owner set the next priority: reading the terrain. The game is at its best when you pick a line, commit and feel the truck respond, so the ground should give clues before you commit, enough to stop, look and make an informed call, and not a glowing route. Four ideas came with it (the ground tells the story; the journal as a field notebook; side trips that give information; a finish screen that tells the run's story). They are in `docs/ROADMAP.md` under "Reading the terrain". The owner's pick for one small update was Canal Creek's visual cues, where the written instructions were doing most of the work. This is that update.

**Nothing a wheel meets has changed.** The bed, the depths, the shelf, the holes, the current and both exits are what they were. Only what is drawn, and what the hint and the note say.

**Before.** From the ramp the creek was one grey-green band. The shelf was a faint paler patch: the water's colour reached its darkest by 0.8 m, so the 0.6 m shelf was two thirds of the way to the colour of the 1.7 m holes beside it, and from a low angle the surface mirrored the sky. Which exit to take came from the sign, the note and the hint.

**1. Clear water** (`CreekDef.clear`, set on Canal Creek only; `src/render/water.ts`). Hema calls it a deep blue-water crossing.

- **The bed shows through the shallows.** Water up to 0.65 m deep is drawn about half as opaque and mirrors less of the sky, fading to ordinary water by 1 m. The shelf's bed is pale gravel (a bar in clear water takes the pale sand tint), so the shelf is a pale strip across the creek.
- **The deep is dark.** The colour reaches its darkest from 1.1 m (0.8 m elsewhere), so the shelf at 0.6 m is under half-way and the creek's ordinary 1.1 m and the 1.7 m holes are fully dark. The colour is worked out corner by corner from the depth, so the shelf's edge follows the bed and not the 1 m squares.
- **Broken water over the shallows.** Where clear, shallow water runs, close-set pale glints and a busier surface: over the shelf and along the foot of the far bank. The deep water beside it lies smooth.
- **It goes when the creek floods.** As the rain lifts the creek and muddies it, the clear shallows fade out. Late on a wet day the creek is brown from bank to bank, and the rocks, the signs and the wheel lines are what is left to read.

**2. Worn wheel lines** (`TrackDef.wheelMarks`, `src/sim/world/wheelMarks.ts`, drawn by `src/render/wheelMarks.ts`). Two dark tyre lines, a wheel track apart, on dry ground only: down the ramp between the two rocks to the water, and out of the water on the far bank, left along its foot and up the winding exit to the top. The old exit has none. They are drawn once when the map is built and are not the churn the player's own wheels leave. The first version was a row of separate blobs every 0.35 m, which read as a tank's tread close up; at 0.22 m and fainter they run together.

**3. The words say how to read it.** The hint was "In between the two rocks, straight across at the sign, then left along the far bank and up". It is now "Read the water: pale and broken is the shelf, dark is deep. Wheel lines show the way out". The note keeps what Hema says (a deep crossing of clear blue water; the old exit to the right washed out into gullies; a winding exit to the left) and then tells the driver to stop on the ramp and read it: pale and broken is the shelf, dark and smooth is deep, and the wet wheel lines on the far bank show the exit in use. The two marker rocks and the two signs stay where they are.

**The test, not yet done.** Watch a new player approach without coaching and ask them to explain the line they picked before they reach the water. If the cues hold, the roadmap lists where they go next (Nolans Brook's shoal, ruts into the bog, the CREB's fords). If they do not, the wording can go back: the old hint is quoted above.

**Looked at** (the game in a browser, low graphics, the short 40, the commonest day). Before the change: the top of the ramp from the driver's seat and the chase camera, and the creek from above. After: from above (a pale shelf with broken water between two dark holes, the dark creek either side); from eye height at the foot of the ramp (wheel lines down between the rocks, the pale strip straight ahead with dark water left and right, twin lines climbing left on the far bank, the old exit bare); from the driver's seat and the chase camera at the top of the ramp (the same, smaller); the far bank from across the water (the lines leave the water left of the shelf, run along the foot of the bank and curve up); late on a wet day in the rain with the lights on (brown water bank to bank, no shelf showing). The CREB loads and draws with no shader errors. No vehicle body changed, so no studio renders and no second look by a reviewer.

**Tests.** `tests/readableCreek.test.ts` (7): how far water of each depth counts as shallows and how dark it is drawn, clear and not; that only Canal Creek asks for clear water and only its water is marked; the shelf's pale bed, with the depths and the surface under the wheels as they were; the wheel lines (on dry ground, a wheel track either side of the track's line, from the top of the ramp to the water and from the foot of the far bank to the top of the winding exit, leaving the water to the left); none on the old exit between where the exits part and where they join again, and none on any other track; the hint's and the note's wording. The scripted drives of Canal Creek pass unchanged.

**The full suites on v2.24.** Unit: 119 files, 1,111 passed and 2 to do. Browser: 67 tests, 58 passed and 9 failed, all on the roadmap's list (`episode:15`, `m4-tools:15`, `night:4`, `sideTrips:4`, `toolMenu:4`, `touch:82`, `touch:234`, `uiSize:71`, and the flaky `vehicles:13`).
